# Phase 2 · Machines — sources of inspiration, by control type

*For every kind of control and readout in `PLAN.md`, the machines across
history that carried a memorable one: pre-1900 to the 1990s, military,
aviation, marine, rail, studio, hi-fi, radio, synthesizers, test gear, medical,
industrial, automotive, appliances, cameras, watches, arcade, telecom,
computing, Soviet and Japanese and German and British and American. ★ marks
the three to six per code most worth building. Written by five research
agents on 2026-09-20 from the taxonomy; assembled here unedited. Phase 3
(`PLAN-3-STYLES.md`) picks the specific styles from these lists.*

---

# A · Rotary inputs

*Codes A1–A20 from PLAN.md. One row per machine; ★ marks the 3–6 per code most
worth building as web mock-ups (distinctive, visually rich, feasible in
CSS/SVG). Years marked ~ are approximate. Where a machine is famous for a
different code than the one it sits under, the row says so.*

## A1 · Potentiometer knob
| machine | maker / model | year | domain | what the control looks like | why it's distinctive |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Minimoog Model D | Moog Music | 1970–81 | synth | Black phenolic skirtless knob ~19 mm with a flat top and a single white radial line; the 0–10 scale is silk-screened white on the black panel around it, in a plain grotesk; walnut cheeks either side | The knob is plain — the panel does the talking; the white line-on-black plus wood is *the* synth look |
| MS-20 | Korg | 1978–83 | synth | Black cylindrical knob with a raised white "hat" pointer ridge running from centre to rim, ~16 mm; graduated 0–10 arc printed on the black panel in white; patch-jack field to the right | The raised pointer ridge you can find with a fingertip; instantly Korg |
| Prophet-5 rev 3 | Sequential Circuits | 1978–84 | synth | Small black Rogan knob with a shallow dome and a white pointer dot near the rim; Koa-wood end cheeks; grey panel with fine white legend | The dot rather than a line; the knobs sit in dense rows over an aluminium panel |
| TB-303 Bass Line | Roland | 1982–84 | synth | Silver-grey cylindrical knobs with a black rubber band and a black pointer line; the panel is silver with a black scale; 6 in a row above the step buttons | The two-tone silver/black band; the tiny size |
| Space Echo RE-201 | Roland | 1974–~90 | studio / stage | Large black knobs with a bright chrome-look pointer line, on a dark green panel with white legends; the "Repeat Rate", "Intensity" etc. sit above the tape-loop window | Green panel + chrome line = Roland 70s; the mode selector beside them is A5 |
| DS-1 Distortion / Boss compact pedals | Boss | 1977→ | stage | Small black knobs ~14 mm with a white pointer line and a soft-knurled rim, set into the pedal's stamped top ahead of the treadle; legends in condensed caps on the coloured (orange, yellow, blue) enamel | The three-across knob row under the treadle; any guitarist knows it by silhouette |
| Phase 90 | MXR | 1974→ | stage | A single large black skirted knob ("SPEED") on a bright orange box, white script legend | One knob, one colour; the minimal case |
| Fuzz Face | Dallas Arbiter | 1966–~75 | stage | Two black knobs on a round grey or red cast case, arranged as eyes above the switch "mouth" | The face; the round enclosure |
| 1073 module | Neve | 1970→ | studio | Grey, red and blue "Marconi-style" knobs: tall, waisted, bulbous at the base, with a moulded pointer nose; grey/blue anodised panel with white engraved legend | The colour-coded waisted knob on an 80-series desk; the stepped switches beneath are A19 |
| SL 4000 E console | Solid State Logic | 1979→ | studio | Dozens of small (~11 mm) soft-touch knobs per channel, colour-coded (grey, blue, red, green, yellow, brown) with a white dot pointer; grey panel with black legends | The colour code by function across a wall of knobs; the dot pointer |
| 512c / 550A modules | API | 1968→ | studio | Black knob with a broad skirt and a white pointer line; blue-grey 500-series panels, black legend in a bold grotesk | The skirt-to-cap ratio; the "API knob" is still made unchanged |
| 1176LN limiter | Universal Audio / UREI | 1967→ | studio | Large black knob with a fine-knurled rim and a white index line; silver-grey brushed panel with a black legend; the four ratio buttons are D2 | The big knurled knob next to the VU; the line that reads from across the room |
| SK 4 "Snow White's Coffin" | Braun (Rams / Gugelot) | 1956 | hi-fi | Small pale-grey cylindrical knobs set into the white sheet-steel top with a fine black hairline and the scale printed *on the panel* in Akzidenz Grotesk; the Lucite lid over the turntable | The knob is a pure cylinder; the legend does the pointing; the first "Rams knob" |
| T 1000 world receiver | Braun (Rams) | 1963–68 | radio | Machined aluminium knobs, clear-anodised, fluted rim, black inset top; grey panel; lettering in Akzidenz Grotesk | Metal knobs on a grey-and-aluminium field; the CSV/TS-45 family shares them |
| Model 2270 receiver | Marantz | 1971–75 | hi-fi | Brushed-aluminium knobs with a black scale ring and a domed silver centre; the big tuning knob is a "Gyro-Touch" flywheel; blue-lit dial glass | Aluminium-on-aluminium with the blue dial; the flywheel spin |
| SX-1980 receiver | Pioneer | 1978–80 | hi-fi | Big fluted brushed-aluminium knobs, black scale ring with fine white graduations, a black line index; a Helvetica-style legend below | Peak of the silver-face era; knobs the size of a coffee cup lid |
| C22 / MC275 | McIntosh | 1963→ | hi-fi | Black knobs with a knurled rim and a machined silver cap, on black glass with gold/green lettering (Bulova-style serif caps) | Black glass and gold legend; the knobs are matte on gloss |
| Beolit 39 / Beomaster 900 | Bang & Olufsen | 1938 / 1964 | radio / hi-fi | 1939: dark brown Bakelite knobs with concentric ridges on a streamlined Bakelite cabinet; 1964: flat teak-sided receiver with brushed-metal cylindrical knobs and a printed grey scale | The Bakelite ridges vs the flat Danish aluminium; two eras of the same house |
| Sølvsuper 10 | Tandberg | ~1962 | radio | Big black knobs with a chrome-plated rim ring and a white index dot, teak cabinet, cream dial glass with green pointer | The chrome ring on a black knob; the "Sølvsuper" script |
| Kurér portable | Radionette (Norway) | 1950–~60 | radio | Cream Bakelite knobs, gently conical with a black pointer stripe, on a tan Bakelite case with a fabric grille | The conical cream knob on a "Kurér" case that every Norwegian family owned |
| TS 502 cube radio | Brionvega (Zanuso / Sapper) | 1964 | radio | Two large flat cylindrical knobs (volume, tuning) set into one half of a hinged plastic cube; orange, yellow, white or black moulded ABS; the knob top carries the numeral ring | Knob is the whole face of the cube; colour is the finish |
| RR126 radiofonografo | Brionvega (Castiglioni) | 1965 | hi-fi | Two large white knobs with black pointer dots, arranged as eyes on a "face" with the dial as a mouth; cube on castors | Anthropomorphic layout; the knob as an eye |
| Zvezda-54 (Звезда-54) | Kharkiv radio plant (USSR) | 1954 | radio | Cream and gilt Bakelite knobs on a red-and-gold star-shaped cabinet (copy of the French Excelsior 52) | The star cabinet; gilt knobs on red |
| Okean-209 (Океан-209) | Minsk Radio (USSR) | 1976–~80 | radio | Large black plastic knobs with a fine-ribbed grip and a white pointer, Cyrillic legends (ГРОМК., ТЕМБР) in a bold grotesk, black-and-chrome case | Chunky Soviet portable; Cyrillic condensed caps |
| R-123M tank radio (Р-123М) | USSR | ~1965–80 | military | Grey-green hammertone box; large black Bakelite knobs with a radial pointer and knurled rim; bright yellow-white Cyrillic engraved legend; a red-lit dial window | Grey-green, yellow Cyrillic, red window; Warsaw-Pact vehicle radio look |
| Wireless Set No. 19 Mk II | Pye / Northern Electric (UK/Canada) | 1941–45 | military | Black wrinkle-paint front; black Bakelite knobs with moulded pointers; legends in *both* English and Cyrillic (Lend-Lease sets) | Bilingual legend on a WWII tank set — a ready-made period detail |
| S-38 / SX-28 | Hallicrafters | 1946 / 1940 | radio (ham) | Black Bakelite knobs with the "h" in a circle moulded into the cap, chrome centre dot; grey wrinkle or black panel with silver legend | The moulded monogram; the SX-28's twin dials |
| KWM-2 | Collins Radio | 1959–~78 | radio (ham) | Black fluted knobs with a bright aluminium skirt and a fine hairline; the round-cornered "Collins S-line" panel in grey-green with white legend | The fluted black + aluminium skirt is the "Collins knob"; the S-Line panel radius |
| SB-101 / HW-101 | Heathkit | 1966–~76 | radio (ham) | Two-tone knobs: black cap with a satin aluminium skirt, red pointer on the main tuning; green-grey two-tone panel | The green-grey Heathkit panel with two-tone knobs; the kit-built look |
| TS-520 | Kenwood / Trio | 1973–78 | radio (ham) | Large black main-tuning knob with a finger dimple and a silver ring; small knobs with white lines; Eurostile-ish legend on grey | Finger dimple; the 70s Japanese transceiver look |
| Type 535A / 545 oscilloscope | Tektronix | 1954–~65 | test | Black Bakelite knobs with an aluminium skirt and a red concentric "VARIABLE" cap; grey-blue crackle cabinet; legend engraved in white | The red inner knob inside a black outer — the Tek look for 30 years |
| 465 oscilloscope | Tektronix | 1972–~83 | test | Blue-grey panel; black knobs with a white line; red "VAR" concentric cap; "UNCAL" lamp beside | Blue-grey plus red cap; the most-seen bench scope |
| 200CD audio oscillator | Hewlett-Packard | 1952–~85 | test | Large black knob with a white pointer and an engraved aluminium dial skirt; grey wrinkle cabinet; legend in stencil-like caps | The big black knob on wrinkle-grey; HP's first look |
| 8640B signal generator | Hewlett-Packard | 1973–~85 | test | Grey knobs with a black rim and a white line; the huge central tuning knob with a lock lever; dark-grey panel with white Helvetica | The big tuning knob on the 70s HP grey |
| Model 8 Avometer | AVO (UK) | 1951–~2008 | test | Two very large black Bakelite knobs with moulded pointer bars, a black Bakelite case, cream scale card (the range switches are A5) | The twin knobs bigger than a hand's width; a British lab icon |
| Simpson 260 | Simpson Electric | 1936→ | test | Black Bakelite selector (A5) and a smaller black "ZERO OHMS" knob with an engraved white line; cream face with red/black scales | The ZERO OHMS knob next to the big selector; the Bakelite pointer |
| Model 630 | Triplett | 1955–~90 | test | Red Bakelite selector knob with a white pointer on a black face | The one red knob |
| TF 144H signal generator | Marconi Instruments | ~1955–65 | test | Grey wrinkle cabinet; black knobs with a white line and a chrome cap; engraved black filled lettering | The Marconi grey wrinkle finish |
| G3-33 (Г3-33) audio generator | USSR | ~1970s | test | Black Bakelite knobs with a moulded pointer on a pale grey enamel panel; Cyrillic condensed grotesk; the big central frequency dial | Soviet lab grey; the Cyrillic legend |
| Mark 7 respirator | Bird Corporation | 1955–~80 | medical | Green anodised knobs (pressure, flow, sensitivity) on a green-and-chrome body with a clear acrylic chamber | Green on chrome; medical 50s |
| Model 5375 external pacemaker | Medtronic | ~1970s | medical | Small grey knobs with black pointers ("RATE", "OUTPUT") on a cream enamel box with the Medtronic rising-figure logo | The tiny knobs on a life-critical box |
| Model 40 pneumatic controller | Foxboro | ~1950s–70s | industrial | Black Bakelite "SET POINT" knob with a white pointer on a grey steel case with a cream chart | Process-control grey; the pointer knob next to a circular chart |
| Etch A Sketch | Ohio Art | 1960→ | toy | Two white cylindrical knobs with a shallow dome, on a red plastic frame with the gold script logo | Two knobs on red; a universal image |
| Paddle controller CX30 | Atari | 1977–~84 | games | Black cylindrical knob with a fine-ribbed grip on a black plastic body with an orange button | The knob as the entire controller |
| Odyssey | Magnavox | 1972 | games | Three black knobs (horizontal, vertical, English) on a white-and-brown plastic controller box | The three-knob paddle |
| Mixmaster | Sunbeam | 1930→ | domestic | A round numbered speed dial (1–10 or 1–12) on the top of the mixer head, cream Bakelite with black numerals | The speed dial on the head; the numerals on the knob itself |
| KM 3 kitchen machine | Braun (Rams / Seiffert) | 1957–~93 | domestic | White cylindrical knob with a coloured pointer dot on a white body | Rams white-on-white; the dot |
| Rotary dimmer | Lutron (Joel Spira) | 1961→ | domestic | Ivory or brown plastic push-on/push-off knob on a wall plate, plain cylinder with a shallow dome (see also A20) | The wall knob; universally recognised |
| Bel Air dash radio knobs | General Motors / Delco | 1957 | automotive | Chrome-plated knobs with a black inset face on a chrome-and-turquoise dash; script legends | Chrome on chrome; the 50s dashboard |
| W123 heater controls | Mercedes-Benz | 1976–85 | automotive | Chunky black plastic wheels with a white index on a dark dash; pictogram legends in white | The wheel-knob; Mercedes' black plastic |
| Trabant 601 dash | VEB Sachsenring (DDR) | 1964–90 | automotive | Plain black plastic knobs with white stamped symbols on a painted steel dash | The absolute minimum; East German pragmatism |
| Command Module main display console | North American Rockwell (Apollo) | 1968–72 | spacecraft | Grey panel; rotary switches with black bar-pointer knobs (MS91528-type), white engraved legend in Futura-like caps | Almost all knobs are A2/A5 selectors; the panel grey and legend style are the source |
| Apollo/Shuttle MIL-spec knob (MS91528) | US DoD standard | 1950s→ | military | Black phenolic bar or round knob with a white pointer and set-screw; used on every US aircraft, ship and console for 50 years | A single standard that unifies military panels — build it once, reuse everywhere |
| Portastudio 144 / 244 | Tascam | 1979 / 1982 | studio | Rows of small black knobs with a white dot, on a black panel with orange/blue trim lines; sliders below | The four-channel knob rows over faders on a home studio box |
| A77 tape recorder | Revox / Studer | 1967–77 | studio | Large grey-black collet-style knobs (A4) and small aluminium knobs with a black line; brushed-aluminium panel | Swiss; the big function knobs beside the VU meters |
| Nagra IV-S | Kudelski | 1971–~2000 | broadcast | Small black knobs with a white line beside the big main function selector; grey panel; engraved white legend | Tiny knobs next to a lever selector; the modulometer above |

Build candidates: ★ Minimoog (black knob, white line, scale on panel) · ★ Korg MS-20 hat-pointer knob · ★ Tektronix 535A / 465 black-with-red-VAR concentric · ★ Braun SK 4 pale-grey cylinder with hairline · ★ SSL 4000 E colour-coded dot knobs · ★ R-123M Soviet Bakelite pointer on grey-green with yellow Cyrillic · ★ MS91528 MIL-spec bar knob (reusable)

## A2 · Chicken-head / pointer knob
| machine | maker / model | year | domain | what the control looks like | why it's distinctive |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 5E3 Deluxe / 5F6-A Bassman (tweed era) | Fender | 1955–60 | stage | Black Bakelite "chicken head" (Daka-Ware type) ~30 mm long: wedge pointer with a flat top, moulded on a boss with a set-screw; chrome control plate with black stamped legends; tweed cabinet | The archetype: chicken head on chrome on tweed |
| AC30 | Vox (JMI) | 1960–~68 | stage | Black chicken-head knobs on a copper- or gold-coloured control panel with a red "Vox" logo, scale 0–10 printed on the panel | Chicken heads on copper; the top-mounted panel |
| EQP-1A equaliser | Pultec | 1951–~70 | studio | Black Bakelite pointer knobs (Daka-Ware) on a cream/grey panel with red and black engraved legend; frequency selectors and boost/atten pots use the same knob | The cream panel + black pointers; every "vintage EQ" plug-in copies it |
| LA-2A leveling amplifier | Teletronix / UA | 1965→ | studio | Two big black Bakelite pointer knobs ("GAIN", "PEAK REDUCTION") on a grey panel, white legend, one VU centred | The two-knob face |
| Copicat echo | Watkins (WEM) | 1958–~80 | stage | Cream/black chicken-head knobs on a black or hammertone box with red WEM script | The chicken head in Britain |
| Uni-Vibe | Shin-ei / Univox | 1968–~72 | stage | Black chicken-head knobs on a dark blue-grey box with white legend | Chicken head as "vintage stage" shorthand |
| 351 tape recorder electronics | Ampex | 1958–~65 | studio | Black pointer knobs with a chrome insert on a grey-beige panel; the VU meter alongside | Pointer knob on the classic recorder |
| 76-B broadcast console | RCA | 1946–~55 | broadcast | Large black Bakelite pointer knobs on a grey-blue panel with white numbered scales; the pointers are long bars with a chamfered nose | Long-bar pointers in a row; the broadcast look before rotary faders |
| Uniselector / GPO test racks | GPO / BPO (UK) | 1930s–60s | telecom | Brown Bakelite pointer knobs, cream scale plates, engraved-fill black | The brown Bakelite of British telecom |
| T1154 transmitter | Marconi (Air Ministry) | 1940–45 | military | Large Bakelite pointer knobs in **red, blue and yellow** for the three wave-bands, on a black wrinkle panel with white engraved legend | Coloured pointer knobs on a Lancaster's transmitter; the colour code |
| R1155 receiver | Marconi | 1940–45 | military | Black Bakelite pointer knobs, two panel meters, a large calibrated dial; black wrinkle finish | The Bomber Command receiver; the pointer knobs among many |
| B-17 / B-24 radio compass control box | Bendix (MN-26 / BC-433) | 1940–45 | military | Black crackle box; black pointer knobs with an ivory-white index; "REC – LOOP – COMP – ANT" legend | Wartime crackle black with pointer knob |
| Command Module rotary switches | Apollo (NAR) | 1968–72 | spacecraft | Machined MS91528-style black bar knobs with a white nose; positions engraved white on grey | The NASA bar-pointer on rotaries |
| Space Shuttle forward panel | Rockwell | 1981–2011 | spacecraft | Same MIL-spec bar knobs, grey panels, black legend; the pointer nose is painted white | Continuity from Apollo; the white nose |
| Uher 4000 Report | Uher | 1961–~80 | broadcast | Small black pointer knobs on a grey-blue portable; a numbered scale on the panel | Compact reporter's recorder; the mini pointer |
| Selmer Truvoice Treble-n-Bass | Selmer (UK) | 1962–~68 | stage | Black pointer knobs on a red "croc-skin" covered cabinet | Croc skin + pointer |
| Moto-Tool 380 | Dremel | ~1975–85 | industrial | Black pointer speed knob on a grey plastic body, numbered 1–5 on the housing | Pointer knob on a hand tool; the domestic end of the type |

Build candidates: ★ Fender tweed chicken head on a chrome plate · ★ Pultec cream-panel pointer · ★ T1154 red/blue/yellow coloured pointers · ★ RCA 76-B long bar pointer · ★ MS91528 bar knob (shared with A1)

## A3 · Skirted knob with numbered skirt
| machine | maker / model | year | domain | what the control looks like | why it's distinctive |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Stratocaster | Fender | 1954→ | instrument | White or aged-cream plastic "bell" knob, ~20 mm, with a numbered skirt 1–10 in black, "VOLUME" / "TONE" on the top; three in a diagonal row on a white pickguard | The most-seen numbered skirt in the world; the numerals in a plain condensed grotesk |
| Telecaster | Fender | 1951→ | instrument | Chrome-plated dome knobs with a knurled side (no numbers) on a chrome control plate | Contrast to the Strat: knurled metal, unnumbered |
| Les Paul "top hat" / "reflector" knobs | Gibson | ~1955→ | instrument | Gold or amber transparent top-hat knob with a numbered skirt 0–10 and a silver or gold "reflector" cap ("VOLUME"/"TONE" printed) | The see-through amber and the reflector disc |
| Les Paul "speed knob" | Gibson | 1952–55 | instrument | Barrel-shaped gold-top knob, numbered 0–10 around the rim | The barrel |
| Twin Reverb / Deluxe Reverb (blackface) | Fender | 1963–67 | stage | Black plastic skirted knob with a white numbered skirt 1–10, a white index line, a chrome-look centre cap; black panel with white legend | The numbered skirt on a black amp panel — the "Fender amp knob" |
| JTM45 / 1959 "Plexi" | Marshall | 1962–69 | stage | Black knob with a gold-coloured numbered skirt 0–10 and a gold cap, on a gold-backed Plexiglas panel | Gold plexi + gold-skirt knob; JMP era went black-on-black |
| JCM800 | Marshall | 1981–~91 | stage | Black skirted knob 0–10 with a white index, on a gold-anodised panel | The 80s Marshall panel |
| OR120 / "Pics Only" | Orange | 1972–~79 | stage | Black skirted knobs with a white line beside pictogram (hieroglyph) legends on an orange panel | The pictograms |
| SVT | Ampeg | 1969→ | stage | Black knurled knobs with a numbered chrome-look skirt; blue-lit panel lamps | The blue and the skirt |
| 1484 "Twin Twelve" | Silvertone (Sears) | 1963–67 | stage | Black knobs with a chrome cap and numbered skirt | The chrome cap on a grey-vinyl amp |
| Mesa/Boogie Mark IIC+ | Mesa Engineering | 1983–85 | stage | Black skirted knobs 0–10 with a white line; many are push-pull ("PULL BRIGHT") — see A20 | The dense row of pull-function knobs |
| Chef A701 | Kenwood (UK) | 1962–~76 | domestic | Cream plastic knob with a numbered skirt 0–5 and a "P" pulse position, on a cream enamel body | Skirt on a kitchen machine |
| Sunbeam T-20 toaster | Sunbeam | 1949–~60 | domestic | Chrome-plated knob with a black numbered skirt "LIGHT … DARK" | Toaster dial with a skirt |
| Variac W5 / W10 | General Radio | 1934→ | test | Big black Bakelite knob with a 0–140 V numbered skirt behind a fixed pointer, on a black cylindrical autotransformer | The Variac skirt (0–140) is a lab classic |
| Type 500 / 3 series | Elma ("Zoom" numbered dial) | 1970s→ | test | A collet knob (A4) with a numbered skirt disc 0–10 in black on satin aluminium, fine hairline | The engineer's numbered skirt; used on Neve, Studer, EMT |
| Model 30 / 35 | Rogan knobs (used on Prophet / Oberheim / Rhodes) | 1970s | synth | Black skirted knob with a white pointer and a numbered skirt on some Rhodes Mark I/II preamps | The skirt on an electric piano |
| RE-201 Space Echo mode selector | Roland | 1974 | studio | Big black knob with a **numbered skirt 1–12** around a black cap, on green | The numbered mode dial — actually a 12-position A5 with an A3 skirt |
| JC-120 Jazz Chorus | Roland | 1975→ | stage | Black skirted knobs 0–10 with a white line, on a black panel with a silver stripe | Black-on-black with the silver stripe |
| DX7 / CS-80 | Yamaha | 1977 / 1983 | synth | CS-80: a black skirted "Volume" knob with a numbered skirt; DX7: data slider only | The one skirted knob on an otherwise slider synth |

Build candidates: ★ Fender Stratocaster bell knob (white 1–10 skirt) · ★ Fender blackface amp knob (black/white numbered skirt) · ★ Gibson amber top-hat reflector · ★ Marshall Plexi gold skirt · ★ Variac 0–140 skirt · ★ RE-201 1–12 mode dial

## A5 · Rotary selector switch, detented
| machine | maker / model | year | domain | what the control looks like | why it's distinctive |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Simpson 260 | Simpson Electric | 1936→ | test | Very large black Bakelite selector with a long moulded pointer bar, ~50 mm sweep; ranges printed in red (AC) and black (DC) around it on a cream Bakelite face | The bar pointer over red-and-black ranges; the meter's whole personality |
| Model 8 Avometer | AVO | 1951→ | test | Two big black Bakelite range knobs with a raised pointer bar, arranged left/right; white engraved fill; "AVO" at the top | Two selectors that must agree; the pointer bars |
| 535A / 545 / 465 "VOLTS/DIV" and "TIME/DIV" | Tektronix | 1954→ | test | Black outer knob with a fluted skirt, red concentric "VARIABLE" inside; positions (e.g. .01 … 5 V) engraved white on the panel in a 1-2-5 sequence; "UNCAL" lamp | 1-2-5 sequence around a knob with a red centre; the snap is loud |
| 8020A DMM | Fluke | 1977–~85 | test | Grey rotary function switch with a black legend ring (V~, V=, kΩ, mA) on a yellow-and-grey body | The Fluke yellow with a grey switch |
| 77 / 87 DMM | Fluke | 1983 / 1988 | test | Big grey rotary with a white pointer through a legend ring of symbols on a grey/yellow body | The handheld meter switch everyone has held |

# B · Linear inputs, C · Switches

*Slice: B1–B12 and C1–C18 of `PLAN.md`. One table per code; ★ marks the
rows most worth building as CSS/SVG mock-ups (distinctive, visually rich,
feasible). Years marked ~ are from memory and unverified; maker/model names
are given as exactly as recall allows. Cross-references in brackets point at
other codes where the same object also earns a place.*

| Church organ drawstops ★ | Harrison & Harrison, Henry Willis, Cavaillé-Coll, Walker | 1850s– | church organ | turned ivory, ebony or rosewood knobs (~30 mm) on wooden rods, the stop name engraved on an ivory face ("OPEN DIAPASON 8") in a jamb of stops either side of the manuals; pulled *toward* the player | the ivory knob with the engraved name; rows of them in the terraced jambs |
| Harmonium / reed organ stops ★ | Estey, Mason & Hamlin, Alexandre | 1860s–1920s | domestic organ | a row of stop knobs above the keyboard, turned wood with paper or celluloid faces ("VOX HUMANA", "DIAPASON", "FORTE") in ornamental Victorian lettering | the Victorian name plates on a pull knob |

Build candidates: ★ Hammond B-3 drawbar (brown/white/black, numbered stem), ★ Vox Continental grey bar, ★ church organ ivory drawstop with engraved name, ★ harmonium Victorian stop knob, ★ Nord Electro LED-ladder drawbar (as an output pair).

| Martin-Baker seat firing handle ★ | Martin-Baker Mk 4 / Mk 10 ejection seat | 1950s– | military aviation | a black-and-yellow striped loop handle between the pilot's knees (and the earlier face-blind handle above the head) | the hazard-striped loop |
| Boeing 747 / 737 engine fire handles | Boeing | 1968– | airliner | four lit T-handles on the pedestal, pull to arm then rotate to discharge the bottle; red glow when the loop trips | pull then twist, and it lights |

## C17 · Magnetic reed / proximity / tilt (no visible actuator)

Skipped as PLAN.md suggests. Noted only for completeness: the Sentrol / Ademco door-contact reed pair on alarm frames (1970s–, a white plastic pair, one with the magnet); the pinball tilt bob (a plumb bob in a ring, 1930s–); the Nintendo Wii / smartphone accelerometer (2006–) with no actuator at all. None has anything to draw except the housing; build only if a source panel demands it.

# D · Buttons, E · Compound and gestural inputs

*Slice of `PLAN-2-MACHINES.md`, written 2026-09-20 from the Phase 1 taxonomy in `PLAN.md`. One table per code. Years marked ~ are approximate. ★ marks the 3–6 sources per code most worth building as CSS/SVG mock-ups (recognisable, visually rich, feasible). Model names verified where it was cheap: Korry 318/319, Atari 4.5" and 2.25" Trak-Balls, Speak & Spell 1978 vs 1980 keyboards, DSKY 19-key layout, EMS 16×16 matrix and 2k7 pins, Trimphone 766, Dewhurst US91.*

---

## D1 · Momentary pushbutton, domed (doorbell, Bakelite, chrome)

| machine | maker / model | year | domain | what the control looks like | why it's distinctive |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Brass doorbell push | Edwardian "bell push", many UK foundries; the US "Bryant" pushes | ~1890–1920 | domestic / pre-1900 | Round or oval cast-brass escutcheon 50–70 mm, screwed to a door frame, with a mother-of-pearl or porcelain dome ~12 mm in the centre; "PRESS" engraved on some | The pearl dome in a brass rosette: the original momentary button, polished by a century of thumbs |
| Morse key (straight key) | J.H. Bunnell & Co. "Triumph" / Vibroplex "Original" bug | ~1881– / 1905– | telegraph | Not a dome but the ancestor: a black hard-rubber knob ~25 mm on a brass lever, gap-adjust thumbscrews, on a slate or wooden base | The knob's skirt (the "navy knob") and the brass lever's spring: momentary as a gesture |
| Lift/ship "bell push" | Otis brass car-station, Dixon | ~1900–1930 | elevators | Oval brass plate with two or three ivory or black Bakelite domes, "UP", "DOWN", "STOP" engraved and black-filled | The engraved fill lettering and the ivory-yellowed domes |
| Bakelite domed push | Crabtree, MK (UK) bell push; Bryant 1930s (US) | ~1930s | domestic / industrial | Black or brown Bakelite rosette with a raised dome centre; sometimes a luminous ring | The moulding seams and the depth of the phenolic; the button is the same material as the housing |
| Radio "push" (record changer, reject) | Garrard RC series, Wurlitzer 1015 jukebox "reject" | ~1946–1950 | hi-fi / jukebox | Chrome-plated brass dome ~15 mm in a chrome bezel; on the Wurlitzer, backlit plastic keys beside it | The chrome dome's single hot highlight against a dark grille |
| Car horn / starter button | Lucas and Bosch dash starter buttons; the Bentley/Rolls "starter" | ~1930s–1950s | automotive | Black Bakelite dome 20 mm with "START" moulded, in a chrome escutcheon; later the chrome-capped "S" button | The moulded legend rising from the dome; chrome collar with a knurled lock ring |
| Dictaphone / intercom push | Western Electric 6-button "key" telephones (1A2 system) call button; Executone intercoms | ~1950s–60s | telecom | Clear-capped dome buttons with a coloured paper insert; on intercoms, black Bakelite domes with a white ring | Paper legend under a clear dome: a print-your-own label |
| Aircraft press-to-talk | Grimes / Cutler-Hammer PTT on a control yoke or throttle; the B-8 stick's trigger-guard buttons | ~1942–1970s | military / aviation | Black rubber-covered or bare aluminium dome on the yoke horn, 12–15 mm, with a rubber boot | The rubber boot and the wear polish on the yoke; "PTT" stencilled |
| Braun T3 / T4 / T41 radio "bandwahl" buttons | Braun (Dieter Rams) T 41 | 1962 | radio / hi-fi | Small round white or grey plastic domes, ~6 mm, in a strict row; legends in Akzidenz-Grotesk beside them | Tiny, all-identical, spaced on a grid: the Rams idiom that Apple later copied |
| Elevator "hall call" nipple push | Otis "Series 1" halo button precursor; the 1950s Otis hall station | ~1950s | elevators | Small stainless-steel dome nipple in a flush stainless plate, "UP"/"DOWN" arrows engraved | The nipple's tiny reflected-light band on brushed steel |
| Tektronix / HP "beam finder" push | Tektronix 465 (BEAM FIND), HP 180-series | ~1972 | test equipment | Small black rubber-sleeved dome, ~8 mm, on an aluminium panel, legend silk-screened below in Futura/Helvetica | The rubber sleeve on a metal button, panel-mounted with a chrome nut |
| Arcade/pinball "start" chrome push | Bally/Williams cabinet START (pre-1978 electromechanical era) | ~1960s–70s | pinball | Chrome-plated round push ~19 mm with a clear plastic dome, in a lock-nut collar; later red plastic domes | Chrome nut ring and clear dome: the "credit button" |

Build candidates: ★ Edwardian brass-and-pearl doorbell push (pre-1900 dome in a rosette); ★ 1930s Bakelite bell push (moulded phenolic dome); ★ Lucas "START" Bakelite starter button in a chrome escutcheon; ★ Braun T 41 white dome row (Rams grid); ★ Western Electric 1A2 clear-dome key-telephone buttons with paper inserts.

| Western Electric 1A2 key set (WE 565 telephone) line buttons | Western Electric | 1958–1980s | telecom | A row of square clear-capped buttons ~13 mm with coloured paper inserts (clear/white line, red HOLD) in a black or ivory bezel strip | The red HOLD key at the left; lamps behind the clear caps flash |
| Nagra IV-S "Speed & Pilot" and the Nagra E push controls | Kudelski | 1971 | broadcast / studio | Not the main lever, but small square chrome-collared black caps for "reference oscillator" etc. | Precision-machined feel on a hand-held recorder |

| VT100 keypad "PF1–PF4" (see also D8) | Digital Equipment Corp. | 1978 | computing | Flat-topped square keys with cylindrical dish, grey with black legend | Same key as the keyboard; here in a 4×4 block |

| Siemens / Krone telephone exchange test panel | Siemens & Halske test desks | ~1960s | telecom | Square black Bakelite caps ~12 mm in a chrome collar, engraved white numerals | DIN-engraved lettering in a strict grid |

## D3 · Illuminated pushbutton / annunciator switch (Korry, aircraft, NASA consoles, elevator car buttons)

| machine | maker / model | year | domain | what the control looks like | why it's distinctive |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Boeing 737 / 747 overhead and MIP annunciator switches | Korry 318 (replaced the 319) | 1960s–present | aviation | ½ × 1 inch (0.44 × 0.94 in cutout) rectangular lens, black frame, opaque-black-when-dark legend that reads white, amber or blue only when lit; laser-engraved light plate | The "dead face": the legend is invisible until lit; split upper/lower legends (e.g. "ON" over "FAULT") in two colours |
| Airbus A320 overhead panel | Korry / Esterline switch-lights on the A320 FCU and OHP | 1988 | aviation | Square ~20 mm dead-face pushbuttons in a light-grey panel: "FAULT" (amber, upper) over "ON" (white, lower); "OFF" is white, "AVAIL" green | The Airbus "dark cockpit" philosophy: a dark button means normal |
| Apollo Command Module MDC "MASTER ALARM" and caution panel | North American / Honeywell | 1966–1972 | NASA | Rectangular lit push-to-reset, red "MASTER ALARM" in a black frame; the caution/warning matrix beside it uses translucent white tiles with black legends in Futura | The MASTER ALARM red block; the Futura Demi legend on a matte tile |
| Apollo Mission Control (MOCR) consoles | Philco-Ford console modules, Houston | 1965–1975 | NASA | Rectangular event/status pushbuttons ~25×20 mm, translucent white, amber, green, red plastic with black legend, set in grey aluminium module faces; illuminated from beneath by incandescent lamps | The "Christmas-tree" wall of lit tiles; the pale green console paint |
| Space Shuttle forward flight deck (F6/F8 panels) | Rockwell / Eaton switch-lights | 1981–2011 | NASA | Square lit pushbuttons with a segmented window ("MASTER ALARM" red, "SM ALERT" white), black frame, in a dark-grey panel | Same dead-face idea as Korry; the red "MASTER ALARM" is the most photographed |
| Studer A80 / A800 transport | Studer | 1970 / 1978 | broadcast / studio | Large rectangular translucent caps ~35×20 mm (RECORD red, PLAY green, STOP, FF/RW), lit by incandescent bulbs behind, in a machined grey panel | The lit red RECORD button on a grey tape machine; the caps' frosted depth |
| Ampex ATR-100 transport | Ampex | 1976 | studio | Square black-framed lit push-buttons with white legends; RECORD lit red only when armed | The clean square lens and hairline frame |
| MCI JH-110 / JH-24 remote and transport | MCI | 1973 / 1978 | studio | Rectangular translucent buttons in colours (blue RECORD-READY, etc.) on the autolocator remote | The MCI blue and the chunky rounded rectangle |
| Neve / SSL console switches | SSL 4000E "cut" and "solo"; Neve 80-series lit lamps | 1979 / 1970s | studio | Small square (≈8 mm) push-buttons with an LED cap in green/red/yellow on the SSL; Neve's have a lamp behind a clear cap | The SSL coloured cap: red CUT, green SOLO; the tiny squares in a grid |
| Otis "Series 1" / Otis Lexan halo buttons | Otis Elevator | ~1970s–80s | elevators | Black round Lexan button ~30 mm with a white translucent ring (halo) that lights on registration; numeral moulded in the centre | The halo ring: the definitive lit elevator button |
| Dewhurst US91 | Dewhurst | ~1990s–present | elevators | Stainless round pressel with illuminated tactile legend and an illuminated braille tag; halo at half-brightness, full on call acceptance | Legend and halo lit at two intensities |
| IBM System/360 Model 65 / 91 operator console | IBM | 1965 | computing | Rows of small square translucent buttons/lamps (white, red, yellow) in a black bezel grid; "EMERGENCY PULL" red knob beside | The IBM grid of lit squares; the Univers legends |
| PDP-11/70 front panel (see also C-class) | Digital Equipment Corp. | 1975 | computing | Not lit push-buttons but the purple-and-magenta rocker keys; the lit ones are the "RUN"/"ADDRESS" lamps | DEC's magenta/purple colour block |
| Ballistic missile launch console (Minuteman LCC) | Boeing / Sylvania Minuteman II/III console | 1960s–present | military | Square lit annunciators ("LAUNCH IN PROCESS", "STRATEGIC ALERT") in grey panels, white-on-black dead-face | The dead-face status grid; the key switches beneath (C6) |
| Soviet Soyuz / Buran "Neptun" panel | NPO Molniya / RKK Energia | 1970s–2000s | military / space | Rectangular lit buttons with Cyrillic legends in a Soviet grotesk, ivory-cream panels, the buttons themselves in translucent grey with black text | Cyrillic dead-face legends on cream: instantly Soviet |
| Broadcast "ON AIR" / mixer channel ON button | Ward-Beck, Neve 5316, McCurdy | ~1970s–80s | broadcast | Large square lit ON (red) / OFF (yellow) push pair per channel, translucent lens with engraved legend | The red ON square that the presenter watches |
| Roland TR-909 / TB-303 step and function keys | Roland | 1983 | drum machines | TR-909: rectangular buttons with an LED in the cap corner (not lit from behind); TB-303: tiny lit-by-LED round pushes | LED-in-the-cap rather than lamp behind: the 80s Japanese variant |
| Linn LM-1 / LinnDrum "drum pads" and function keys | Linn Electronics | 1980 / 1982 | drum machines | Rectangular lit push-buttons with a red LED beside; the LinnDrum's black-on-cream | The cream panel and the black-printed drum names |
| Fairlight CMI IIx "Page" keys | Fairlight | 1983 | samplers | The alphanumeric keyboard's function row: rectangular grey keys with LED indicators; the music keyboard's lit "SET" keys | Key + LED combos on the light-grey Fairlight console |

| Allen-Bradley 800T / 800H | Allen-Bradley (Rockwell) 800T-FXQ | ~1950s–present | industrial | 30.5 mm oil-tight, red 57 mm jumbo mushroom head in a chrome-plated bezel, black legend ring; heavy die-cast body | The chrome bezel and the "jumbo" 57 mm head; the 800T's oil-tight gasket look |

| Machine-tool pendant station | Hoffmann / Rittal / Idec pendant, on Bridgeport and Haas mills | ~1970s–present | industrial | Yellow pendant box with a red mushroom at the top, black-and-yellow striped ring, green START and red STOP flush buttons below | The hand-held yellow box with the mushroom on top |
| Rail platform / tube "emergency stop" | London Underground platform stop plungers; German Bahn "Not-Halt" | ~1950s–present | rail | Large red mushroom in a black and yellow diagonally striped plate, often under a break-glass or hinged cover; "EMERGENCY STOP – PENALTY FOR IMPROPER USE" | The hazard-striped plate and the threat text |
| Otis / Schindler "STOP" in a lift car | Otis in-car stop switch (older cars) | ~1960s–80s | elevators | Red mushroom ~30 mm or a red toggle marked STOP/RUN, in the car operating panel, with an alarm bell button beside | The only red thing in a stainless panel |
| Escalator stop | Otis / Schindler / Kone escalator balustrade stop | ~1970s–present | elevators | Red mushroom in a stainless or chrome-plated cup at the newel, "STOP" cast into the cup rim, sometimes with a clear hinged guard | Recessed in a cup so nobody hits it by accident |

| Boeing B-52 / B-47 bomb release and "jettison" | Boeing, Cutler-Hammer switch-lights and guarded pushes | 1950s | military | Red push-button ~19 mm under a hinged red-anodised aluminium guard, "JETTISON" stencilled on the guard; the guard is spring-loaded and lifts with a thumb | The red guard with the stencil legend, in a black wrinkle-paint panel |
| McDonnell Douglas F-4 Phantom "PICKLE" / weapon release | B-8 stick's red bomb-release button and the F-4 armament panel guards | 1960s | military | On the stick: a bare red button on top; on the panel: rectangular clear-plastic guards over lit buttons | The clear guard so the lit legend still reads (see D3 overlap) |
| Minuteman LCC "LAUNCH" | Boeing / Sylvania Minuteman console | 1962–present | military | Not a button but a keyed rotary switch (C6) — included because the imagined "big red button" is really this; the "LAUNCH IN PROCESS" annunciator is the visual | Cultural reference point; two keys 12 feet apart |
| Apollo LM / CM "ABORT" and "ABORT STAGE" | Grumman LM, North American CM | 1968–72 | NASA | Round push-buttons ~25 mm under a hinged clear or black guard; ABORT is a red flip-guard on the CM MDC; LM has two: ABORT and ABORT STAGE, both guarded | The word "ABORT" in Futura Demi on a red guard |

| Naval fire-control "FIRE" push | US Navy Mk 37 director; RN Sea Dart / Sea Wolf consoles; Soviet Project 941 launch panels | 1940s–80s | military / naval | Large chrome or black mushroom-ish push under a red bar guard or hinged cover; "FIRE" engraved | The bar guard (a bail you lift) rather than a hinged cover |
| Soviet / Russian "ПУСК" launch button | R-36 / Topol control desks; Soyuz "СПУСК" | 1960s–present | military / Soviet | Grey or red round button under a clear rectangular hinged guard on a cream panel; "ПУСК" black Cyrillic | Cream-and-Cyrillic again; the clear guard is yellowed acrylic |
| Arcade "coin door" test / service | Atari cabinets, Williams | 1980s | arcade | Small red pushbutton under a hinged flap inside the coin door (not guarded from the player) | Rarely seen but the "hidden button" archetype |
| Industrial press "two-hand" run buttons | Allen-Bradley 800T with "mushroom guard" shrouds; Banner two-hand controls | ~1960s–present | industrial | Two green palm buttons each inside a raised shroud/cup so both hands are needed | The shroud that forces deliberate use |
| Racing-car "KILL" / fire-extinguisher pull | Lifeline / Sparco cockpit fire button; the FIA "E" sticker | ~1980s–present | automotive | Red button under a clear flip-cover or a T-handle pull; red circle "E" decal | The red "E" decal and the clear cover |
| Toggle-guard adjacent (see C2) | Dialight / MS25224 red guard | 1950s– | military | The red spring guard over a toggle, often beside a guarded push | Family resemblance: the red anodised guard |
| Missile Command "launch" fantasy: the DEFCON-style console prop | (film props: WarGames 1983, Dr. Strangelove 1964) | 1964 / 1983 | culture | Big red domed button under a clear plastic hinged cover on grey; the origin of the meme | The clear cover with the finger notch |

## D7 · Typewriter key (glass-topped, round, chrome ring; later the sculpted plastic)

|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Sholes & Glidden / Remington No. 1 | E. Remington & Sons | 1874 | typewriters / pre-1900 | Round keys ~18 mm with the letter on a white disc in a black or nickel metal rim (glass over paper on ~later examples), on long steel stems; no lower-case, so all capitals | The origin of QWERTY and of the glass key |

| Corona 3 folding | Standard Typewriter Co. (Corona) | 1912 | typewriters | Small glass keys, only three rows, "FIG" and "CAP" shift keys | Three rows: a compact variant |
| Oliver No. 9 | Oliver Typewriter Co. | 1915 | typewriters | Glass keys in nickel rings with the legend printed in a heavy sans; green enamelled "batwing" frame | The green frame and downstrike batwings |
| Hermes 3000 | Paillard (Switzerland) | 1958 | typewriters | Sculpted sea-foam green plastic keys ~15 mm, concave, with dark-green legends in a rounded grotesk (Helvetica-like); chrome-free | Sea-foam green everything; the softly domed concave key |
| Olympia SM3 / SM9 | Olympia Werke (West Germany) | 1955 / 1964 | typewriters | Round cream or green sculpted plastic keys with dark legends; grey crinkle-paint body | The cream key on grey; the DIN-ish legend |
| Olivetti Lettera 22 / Lettera 32 | Olivetti (Marcello Nizzoli) | 1950 / 1963 | typewriters | Round sculpted plastic keys in ivory or the Lettera 32's blue-grey; legends in a light sans, the space bar long and shallow | Nizzoli's soft geometry; the Lettera 32's blue-grey |
| Olivetti Valentine | Olivetti (Ettore Sottsass, Perry King) | 1969 | typewriters | Same keys as the Lettera 32 mechanism but in black with white legends, inside the red ABS body with orange ribbon-spool caps; black key rings | Red case, black keys, two orange caps: the pop-art typewriter |
| Smith-Corona Sterling / Silent-Super | Smith-Corona | 1950s | typewriters | Green or two-tone tombstone-shaped plastic keys with white legends; the "Silent-Super" in green | Tombstone key shapes |
| Facit T2 / TP1 | Facit (Sweden) | 1958 | typewriters | Sculpted grey keys with sharp square-ish tops; the "Facit" wordmark on the paper table | Nordic square-cornered key tops |
| Erika / Optima | VEB Erika (East Germany) | 1950s–70s | typewriters | Cream keys with black legends in a DIN-style sans, on a grey or green body | East German DIN legends |
| Brother / Silver-Reed portable | Brother JP-1, Silver-Reed Silverette | 1960s–70s | typewriters | Square-ish sculpted keys in white or beige with black Helvetica legends | The transition to square, computer-like keys |
| Adler / Triumph Gabriele | Adler-Werke | 1960s | typewriters | Square-cornered keys with a slight cylindrical dish, blue-grey body | Cylindrical dish anticipates D8 |

Smith lozenge "SHIFT KEY"; ★ Olympia SM3 cream sculpted key on grey crinkle.

## D8 · Terminal / computer key (Selectric, VT100, Model M, Space Cadet, Cherry)

| machine | maker / model | year | domain | what the control looks like | why it's distinctive |

| Arcade coin door: Atari / Coin Controls / Happ over-under door | Atari / Coin Controls (UK) / Happ | 1978– | arcade | A steel door with two coin slots (top and bottom, or side by side) with a chrome bezel and coin-return buttons (usually red or chrome) and a coin cup; a lit "INSERT COIN" sign; a lock; "25¢" or "10p" decals | The chrome bezel and red return button on a black door |
| Coin Controls "Mech" / Mars electronic (MEI) validator | Coin Controls / Mars Electronics | 1980s | arcade / vending | A slot with a metal shroud and a "reject" button; the coin drops into a plastic mech visible from inside | The reject button |
| Vending: Coca-Cola Vendo 44 / 81 | Vendo Co. | 1956 / 1955 | appliances | A red machine with a chrome coin slot ("5¢ — 10¢"), a coin-return knob, and a lit "ICE COLD" | The chrome slot on Coca-Cola red |
| Vending: Bally / National Vendors "Snack" machine | National Vendors | 1970s | appliances | A slot with a bill validator (later) and a chrome coin-return lever | Bill validator slot |
| Parking meter: Duncan Miller / POM (Park-O-Meter) | Duncan (Rhodes) / POM | 1935– | industrial | A cast-aluminium dome-top meter with a coin slot on the side (5¢/10¢), a turn-knob, and a red "EXPIRED" flag in the window (G13) | The turn-knob and the red flag |
| Laundromat: Speed Queen / Greenwald coin slide | Greenwald Industries | 1960s– | appliances | A horizontal "coin slide" tray with four coin cut-outs that you push in | The push-in coin slide |
| Turnstile: NYC Subway token turnstile (Perey) | Perey Turnstile Co. | 1953–2003 | rail | A brass or steel token slot on top of a turnstile with a "TOKEN" legend; later the MetroCard swipe slot | The token slot on a Perey |
| London Underground / BR ticket gate ("Cubic" / Westinghouse) | Cubic / Westinghouse | 1980s | rail | A yellow-ringed ticket slot that swallows the ticket and returns it on top | Yellow ring |
| Railway ticket machine: "Almex" / NCR / IBM ticket dispensers; British "Autoslot" | Almex (Sweden) | 1960s | rail | A slot with a "TICKET" chrome plate | Chrome ticket |
| Slot machine: Bally "Money Honey" / IGT | Bally | 1963 / 1980s | slot machines | Chrome coin head with a lit "INSERT COIN" and a coin tray (the "hopper" pays out with a clatter) | The hopper tray |

| Pinball coin door: Bally / Williams / Stern | Bally | 1970s–90s | pinball | Two or three chrome-bezel slots with red return buttons; the "pricing card" behind a clear window | Pricing card |

Build candidates: ★ Western Electric 3-slot payphone head (5¢ 10¢ 25¢); ★ GPO Button A / Button B coin box; ★ Atari/Happ arcade coin door with chrome bezel and red return; ★ Mills Liberty Bell cast coin head; ★ Perey subway token turnstile slot; ★ Greenwald coin slide.

| Sequencer "matrix" boards: Moog 960 / ARP 1601 / Roland System 100M 182 | Moog / ARP / Roland | 1969 / 1977 / 1979 | synthesizers | Rows of knobs, not pins; but the 960's "skip" switches per step form a matrix of tiny toggles | Toggles as a matrix |
| Crumar / Elka "rhythm programmer" pin boards; Wurlitzer Sideman (rotating pin drum) | Wurlitzer | 1959 | drum machines | The Sideman's rotating drum with pins that trigger contacts: a matrix as a cylinder | Pin drum |
| Music box cylinder / barrel organ pin barrel | Reuge / Nicole Frères | 1800s | instruments / pre-1900 | A brass cylinder studded with steel pins that pluck a comb; the ancestor of every step sequencer | Pins on a brass barrel |
| Jacquard loom punched-card chain | Jacquard | 1804 | industrial / pre-1900 | A chain of punched cards read by needles: a matrix on cards | The chain of cards |
| Telephone crossbar switch (Western Electric No. 5 Crossbar) | Western Electric | 1948 | telecom | A 10×20 grid of crosspoints with horizontal select bars and vertical hold magnets, visible through the frame | The crossbar itself, mechanical |
| Pin-board diode matrix (military): B-52 / Minuteman "code" plug matrices | Boeing | 1960s | military | A small pin matrix in a locked panel where code pins set a target | Locked pin board |
| Railway signalling "diode matrix" / mechanical locking frame | GRS / Westinghouse Brake & Signal | 1900s–60s | rail | A locking bed of tappets and dogs beneath the lever frame: a mechanical matrix | Mechanical locking |
| Pin matrix in a toy: "Lite-Brite" | Hasbro | 1967 | toys | A black grid of holes lit from behind; coloured translucent pegs | Pegs in a lit grid |
| Battleship / Mastermind peg boards | Milton Bradley / Invicta | 1967 / 1970 | toys | Grids of holes with red and white pegs | Pegs |
| Modular "matrix mixer": Serge, Doepfer A-138m (knob matrix) | Serge / Doepfer | 1970s / 2000s | synthesizers | A grid of knobs 4×4 | Knob matrix |

Build candidates: ★ EMS VCS3 16×16 pin matrix (shared with E6); ★ IBM 402 wired plugboard; ★ Lite-Brite lit peg grid; ★ Wurlitzer Sideman pin drum; ★ WE No. 5 crossbar grid.

---

*End of the D/E slice. Overlaps noted inline: D3↔H3 (annunciators), D6↔E1 (arcade panels), D10↔D9 (rubber calculators), D16↔E14 (touch), E6↔E11 (matrices), E7↔B7 (trays), E15↔A15/C18 (timers, thermostats). Where a single physical object appears under two codes (the EMS matrix, the DSKY, the Missile Command panel) it is intended: build once, list twice.*


---

# F · Needle and pointer outputs, G · Numerals and characters

*Slice F1–F15 and G1–G17 of the Phase 1 taxonomy in `PLAN.md`. Years marked ~ are
approximate. ★ marks the best candidates for a CSS/SVG/JS mock-up: distinctive,
visually rich, and feasible without bitmaps. Entries name a specific machine wherever
possible; where a meter *family* is the recognisable thing (Weston, Sifam) the family
is listed with its best-known application.*

---

## F1 · Moving-coil panel meter
| machine | maker / model | year | domain | what the readout looks like | why it's distinctive |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Weston Model 1 voltmeter | Weston Electrical Instrument Co., Model 1 | 1888–~1910 | lab / early power | Heavy black-japanned iron case, round, with a wide brass bezel and a domed glass; white card scale with hand-lettered Didone (modern-face) numerals, thin black arc; long black needle with a spade end; brass terminals on the top | The first commercial moving-coil meter: the prototype of everything below. The ornate serif scale and the brass-on-black case say "1890s" instantly |
| Weston Model 301 (2½″ round) / Model 601 | Weston, Model 301 | 1917–1970s | panel, radio, test gear | Round black Bakelite flange case ~2.6″ dia., white scale with a black hairline arc, tapered black knife-edge pointer, "WESTON" in a small serif lockup under the pivot; zero-adjust screw in the centre of the glass | The archetypal American panel meter; on every 1930s–50s transmitter and lab supply. The stubby flange with three mounting holes and the "MODEL 301" lettering |
| Weston Model 643 / 741 (aircraft-style) | Weston, Model 643 | ~1935–1950s | radio / rack | Square-flange black Bakelite, ~3½″, with a flat glass; scale card with two arcs (black and red), knife-edge pointer, mirror strip in the scale for parallax | The mirrored anti-parallax strip is the tell; the square flange with rounded corners fixes its era |
| ★ Simpson Model 29 / 27 (the "wide-vue") | Simpson Electric, Model 29 (3½″), 27 (2½″) | ~1948–1990s | industrial, hi-fi kits (Heathkit), ham | Black phenolic case with a sloped, wide-vision bezel that swells outward; white scale, bold black arc, red trace at the top of the range on some; short, slightly spade-shaped pointer; "SIMPSON" in the Simpson slab-serif logo at the bottom of the scale | The bulging Simpson bezel is unmistakable, and the wide scale arc covers ~100° with big Futura-ish numerals |
| Simpson Model 260 (VOM) meter movement | Simpson, 260 series | 1936–present | multimeter | Large ~4½″ rectangular movement in a black phenolic case, with a stacked multi-scale card (Ω top, V and dB below), a mirror band, and a fat black pointer with a red knife tip on some series | The stacked multi-arc scale on a multimeter — the ohms scale crowding toward the left end — is the recognisable pattern of every VOM |
| Triplett Model 630 / Model 320 panel meters | Triplett Electrical Instrument Co. | 1940s–1970s | multimeter / panel | Very similar to Simpson but with a tighter bezel and the Triplett "T" in a circle; the 630 has a large scale with red and black arcs on a cream card | The cream-tinted scale and the circled "T" |
| ★ Sifam AL29 / AL20 "Clear-front" | Sifam Ltd (Torquay, UK) | 1960s–1980s | British hi-fi, broadcast, lab | Rectangular clear-acrylic front, scale printed on white card with Gill Sans / Univers numerals, thin needle with a curved spade near the tip; usually no bezel — the clear moulding *is* the case; a small chrome zero screw front-centre-bottom | The near-invisible case: scale card floating behind a block of clear acrylic. Also seen in the Quad 33/303 era and on BBC gear |
| Ernest Turner Model 200 / 300 series | Ernest Turner Electrical Instruments (High Wycombe) | 1950s–1970s | British broadcast, EMI, Decca | Round or square bakelite flush-mount, cream or white scale, Gill Sans numerals, very thin needle; PPM-style black scales on some | The British studio look: matte black bezel, cream scale, Gill Sans |
| Soviet M4200 / M42300 / M4761 | ЗИП (Zhitomir), Краснодар ЗИП; types М4200, М42300, М4761, М906 | 1960s–1980s | Soviet radio, test gear, industrial | Square black or dark-brown phenolic with a big square flange; scale on a yellowish-cream or white card with a Cyrillic grotesque ("А", "V", "мкА") and the ГОСТ class-mark symbols in the corner; often a bright red needle; a triangle-headed zero screw | The yellowed scale with the row of tiny GOST symbols (class 1.5, ⊥ mounting, ~, the "magnetoelectric" glyph) along the bottom edge |
| RFT / VEB Meßgerätewerk Zwönitz panel meters | RFT (GDR) | 1960s–1980s | East German radio and test gear | Slim, wide rectangular meters in grey or black plastic, scale in DIN-ish sans; the RFT rhombus logo | DIN numerals and the RFT logo; the extra-wide arcs |
| Hoyt / Beede / Jewell "aircraft" meters | Hoyt Electrical Instrument Works | 1930s–1960s | radio, industrial | Round black phenolic, 2″ with a flat glass and an exposed flange ring; small serif or grotesque numerals; sometimes a blue-tinted scale | The small, plain, all-black case; the recognisable "industrial" look in Heathkit and Eico kits |
| Cambridge Instrument Co. Unipivot | Cambridge Instrument Co. | 1920s–1950s | lab | Large (5–7″) mahogany or black-crackle case, a long sweep scale on ivory card, knife-edge pointer, mirror strip; brass terminals labelled "+ −" | The wide, shallow lab scale with a mirror band and engraved brass terminal plate |
| Marconi / AVO Model 7 / Model 8 | AVO Ltd (Automatic Coil Winder & Electrical Equipment Co.), Model 8 | 1951–2008 (Model 8), Model 7 1936 | British multimeter | The huge Bakelite case with a 5″ meter across the top: cream scale, black and red arcs, knife-edge pointer, the "AVO" script on the scale; "Cut-out" reset button under the meter | The scale takes half the face and the needle is enormous; the red "AVO" script and the cream card |
| Kyoritsu / Sanwa / Hioki panel meters | Sanwa Electric Instrument; Hioki | 1960s–1980s | Japanese hi-fi and kits | Square black plastic, ~1″–2″, white scale with a red range band, a chrome or black rectangular bezel; used on Trio/Kenwood, Sansui power supplies | The tiny square meter with a chrome bezel on a black faceplate — the Japanese look of the 1970s |
| McIntosh MC275 / MC2105 (see F2 also) | McIntosh Laboratory | 1961– | hi-fi amplifier | Back-lit **blue** meters in a black glass front panel (glass silk-screened black with clear windows); white numerals and arcs, needle black | The McIntosh blue-lit glass front; borrowed for our F2 as well |

Build candidates: ★ Simpson Model 29 (bulging bezel, big arc), ★ Sifam AL29 clear-front (floating scale card), ★ Weston 301 round Bakelite with zero-screw and knife pointer, ★ Soviet M42300 (yellow card, GOST symbols, red needle), ★ AVO Model 8 (huge cream scale, red script logo).

## F2 · VU meter
| machine | maker / model | year | domain | what the readout looks like | why it's distinctive |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ★ Weston Model 862 "Standard Volume Indicator" | Weston, Model 862 (Type A scale) | 1939– | broadcast / recording | Round black phenolic, 3–4″, ivory-buff scale card ("Weston buff"); lower arc −20…+3 dB, upper arc 0–100 %, red from 0 to +3; slim black pointer; "VU" in a small box; two-tone scale with black and red arcs | *The* VU. The 1939 buff scale with the −20…+3 lower arc and 0–100 upper arc; every later VU copies it |
| RCA / Western Electric console VU (Weston 862 in RCA housings) | RCA BC-2B, Western Electric 1126 | 1940s–1950s | broadcast console | Same Weston movement in a chrome-ringed round bezel set into grey hammertone or brown-crackle panels | The chrome ring against grey wrinkle-paint |
| ★ Ampex 350 / 351 / 440 electronics | Ampex Corp., 350 (1953), 351 (1958), AG-440 (1967) | 1953–1970s | studio tape recorder | Square-flange black bakelite VU (Weston-type) with a buff scale set into the grey Ampex panel next to a big REC/PLAY indicator; on the AG-440, a rectangular VU with a black bezel in a brushed panel | The 350's grey panel with the round VU and the red REC lamp; the AG-440's lit rectangular VU |
| ★ Neve 80-series (8028, 8048, 8068) VU | Neve Electronics; Sifam (later Ernest Turner 703) meters | 1970s | mixing console | Rectangular VU, yellow-cream scale, warm incandescent backlight through the card, black bezel, small red "PEAK" LED under some; ranked in a long bridge | The row of warm-lit cream VUs above the console; the Sifam / Turner buff card |
| ★ Studer A80 / A810 / A820 | Studer (Regensdorf) | 1970–1980s | studio tape recorder | White or light-grey scale with black lettering, very fine needle, set in a grey or dark-grey Studer panel with the red/green transport lights; A810 meters in a slim black surround with a dB scale to +6 | The Swiss cleanliness: a white scale, Helvetica numerals, no buff tint |
| Nakamichi 1000 / 700 / Dragon | Nakamichi, 1000 (1973), Dragon (1982) | 1973–1980s | cassette deck | Large VUs on the 1000 with black scales, white numerals and a warm lamp; the Dragon uses a bar display instead (H5) | The black-scale VU with white lettering, on a brushed black deck: the anti-Weston |
| ★ Teac A-3340S / A-2340 / A-6300 | Teac | 1970s | reel-to-reel | Four square VUs in a row with black bezels, cream scales, warm backlight, red "PEAK" or "REC" LEDs beside them | The 4-meter row on the A-3340S is an icon of 70s home studios |
| Marantz 2270 / 2325 / 2385 | Marantz (Superscope era) | 1971–1979 | hi-fi receiver | Two small rectangular meters with black scales and **blue** backlighting; blue is the Marantz "gyro-touch" era look | The Marantz blue: black scale, blue-white glow; the tuning meter and signal meter side by side |
| ★ McIntosh MC2105 / MC2205 / MC2300 | McIntosh Laboratory | 1970s | power amplifier | Black glass front, meters lit **blue** from behind, white scale arcs with "WATTS" and dB scales, blue field and a black pointer; illuminated legend under the meter | The McIntosh blue-glass meter is the most recognisable meter in hi-fi |
| Pioneer SX-1250 / SX-1980 | Pioneer | 1976–1979 | receiver | Wide window with two horizontal power meters lit warm-white; black scales with white numerals and a blue-lit dial behind the tuning scale | The "monster receiver" lit-window aesthetic: warm meter lamps, blue dial |
| Sansui AU-717 / AU-919; Sansui G-series | Sansui | late 1970s | amplifier | Black-face meters with white and amber numerals, backlit amber; a red segment at the right | Sansui amber against black |
| Sony TC-377 / TC-630 / TC-880 | Sony | late 1960s–1970s | reel-to-reel | Small square VUs with a dark scale and green-white lamps in a brushed aluminium face | The Sony green-tinted meters |
| Revox A77 / B77 | Studer-Revox | 1967 (A77), 1977 (B77) | reel-to-reel | Two square VUs with cream scales and thin black bezels, set into the brushed-aluminium head-block; A77 has the small red peak lamps below | The two VUs between the reels — the definitive home reel-to-reel image |
| Otari MX-5050 | Otari | 1970s–1980s | semi-pro reel | Two lit VUs in a black bezel, cream scale, small toggle switches beneath | The bezel with a stepped inner ring |
| Sifam AL19 / "Sifam VU" | Sifam | 1960s–present | consoles (Neve, Soundcraft, API copies) | Rectangular, buff scale, black lettering, black rectangular frame, warm lamp behind: the modern reference VU | Buff card, curved frame, the "VU" box under the pivot |
| API 500-series / API 1604 console | Automated Processes Inc. | 1970s | console | Small square Sifam VU with a black frame in the brushed API meter bridge | The tight row of small VUs |
| Uher 4000 Report | Uher | 1961–1980s | portable reel | A single tiny VU with a black scale and a green "battery" band, in a brushed aluminium top plate | The tiny VU on a field recorder |
| Nagra IV-S / 4.2 "modulometer" | Kudelski Nagra | 1971– | field recorder | A large single meter with a black scale, white lettering, a fat white needle, and coloured zones; "MODULOMETER" | The Nagra modulometer's white-on-black face and fat needle; peak-reading, not VU |
| Soviet Маяк-205 / Электроника / Ростов-105 reel decks | Маяк (Kiev), Ростов | 1970s–1980s | Soviet hi-fi | Small square meters, yellowish scale, Cyrillic "дБ", red band, often with a green backlight | The Cyrillic dB scale and the yellowed card |
| RFT / Tesla B-115 / B-116 | Tesla (Czechoslovakia) | 1970s–1980s | East-bloc reel | Square meters with a green-tinted scale and a chrome frame | The green tint |

Build candidates: ★ Weston 862 buff-scale round VU (the original), ★ Neve 80-series warm-lit rectangular Sifam, ★ McIntosh blue-glass meter, ★ Teac A-3340S row of four, ★ Ampex 350 grey panel VU with REC lamp, ★ Nagra modulometer (black face, fat white needle).

## F3 · Edgewise / strip meter
| machine | maker / model | year | domain | what the readout looks like | why it's distinctive |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ★ Nakamichi 500 / 550 / 600 / 610 | Nakamichi, 600 (1976) | 1976–1979 | cassette deck | Two edgewise meters, black scales with white numerals, seen through a narrow horizontal slot in the sloped black face; a warm lamp behind; needle tip rises across the slot | The Nakamichi 600's tilted-front design with the slot meters is a famous industrial-design piece |
| ★ Braun TS 45 / TS 501 / Regie 510 / CSV 1000 | Braun (Dieter Rams team) | 1964–1970s | hi-fi | Long thin edgewise meters in white or light-grey housings, with black hairline scales and a tiny red needle; almost no decoration; Akzidenz-Grotesk numerals | The Rams-era white edgewise meter: a line of black ticks in a white slot |
| Aircraft edgewise (e.g. Weston / Kollsman EGT, ammeter, "vertical tape" in Boeing 707) | Weston, Kollsman, Bendix | 1950s–1970s | aircraft | Narrow vertical or horizontal meters with a black face, white figures, and a white pointer tip in a slot; grouped in engine clusters | The cluster of edgewise engine gauges on a 707 / DC-8 panel |
| Sony TA-1130 / TA-3200F / Sony STR-6120 | Sony | late 1960s–1970s | amplifier / receiver | Horizontal edgewise meters, scale on a black strip with white ticks, green-lit; small chrome bezel | Sony's green edgewise |
| Grundig / Telefunken / Uher 1960s tape recorders (e.g. Grundig TK 46, Telefunken M 24) | Grundig, Telefunken | 1960s | tape | A narrow window with a scale strip and a red pointer that shows only its tip; the scale often a coloured strip (green then red) | The tiny red-tipped "Aussteuerung" window on German recorders |
| ★ Weston "Model 1281" edgewise / Sifam "edgewise MR" series | Weston, Sifam | 1960s–1980s | industrial panel, rack | A rectangular plastic case 4″ wide × 1″ tall; scale card wraps around a cylinder so the tip of the pointer tracks a horizontal line; often mirrored red zone at the right | Straight-line-reading meter; stacks in rows of eight in rack panels |
| Soviet М903 / М68501 edgewise | ЗИП | 1970s–1980s | Soviet test gear, Радиотехника amplifiers | Horizontal slot meters with a yellow-white scale and Cyrillic dB, a black-and-red band; on Radiotehnika У-101 amplifiers they are backlit green | The Radiotehnika green slot meters |
| Sansui / Pioneer "linear scale" (e.g. Pioneer CT-F1000, Pioneer SA-9800) | Pioneer | late 1970s | cassette / amp | Long horizontal scale (100+ mm) with a needle from a pivot below the panel, the tip reading against a straight ruler-like dB scale, lit amber | Very wide straight scale with a needle that appears to travel linearly |
| Yamaha CA-2010 / CT-7000 | Yamaha | 1970s | amp / tuner | Horizontal meters with a black scale and white numerals in a silver face; the CT-7000's narrow multi-meter strip | Yamaha's understated slot meter |

Build candidates: ★ Nakamichi 600 slot meter (black scale, warm lamp, tilted), ★ Braun TS 45 white edgewise, ★ Weston/Sifam industrial edgewise in a row.

## F4 · Centre-zero meter
| machine | maker / model | year | domain | what the readout looks like | why it's distinctive |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Wheatstone bridge galvanometer (Leeds & Northrup 2420, Cambridge "spot") | Leeds & Northrup, Cambridge | 1900s–1950s | lab | Round, ivory card with 0 at top-centre, numerals to left and right, a fine pointer; brass or black case | The symmetric scale with "0" at 12 o'clock |
| ★ Lucas / Smiths ammeter, −30…0…+30 | Smiths Industries; Lucas | 1930s–1970s | British cars, motorcycles | Small round chrome-bezel gauge, black face, white "−30 0 +30", "AMPERES" in condensed sans, a white needle from the bottom | The car-dash centre-zero ammeter: charge/discharge |
| ★ Tuning meter on Sony ST-5000 / Kenwood KT-8300 / Marantz 10B | Marantz Model 10B (1964), Kenwood, Sony | 1960s–1970s | FM tuner | A small rectangular meter, centre-zero "TUNING" with a black scale and a white centre mark, lit; the needle swings both ways across a channel-centre index | The FM centre-tune meter next to a signal meter, the two-meter tuner front |
| Revox A76 / B760 tuning | Studer-Revox | 1970s | FM tuner | Centre-zero in a clean white scale with a black bar at centre | Swiss white |
| Wow-and-flutter / azimuth meter — Nakamichi Dragon azimuth display | Nakamichi | 1982 | cassette | A pair of LEDs on the Dragon; but on the Nakamichi 1000ZXL azimuth: a centre-zero meter | Rare; note the ZXL's centre-zero |
| Balance meter on ohmmeter bridges — Tinsley, Cambridge "Spot" | Tinsley | 1930s–1960s | lab | Wide scale −50…0…+50, mirror strip | Long, symmetric |
| ★ Bell & Howell / Kodak exposure meter (Weston Master II/III/IV) | Weston Model 715 / 735 Master | 1939–1960s | photography | Not centre-zero but a needle across a wide scale in a black bakelite case; include for the *Master's* recognisable curved scale with two ranges and the hinged baffle | The Weston Master's chunky black case and gold "WESTON" |
| Laboratory "null" detector: General Radio 1232-A | General Radio | 1960s | lab | Square panel meter with a centre-zero scale in GR's black-and-grey | The GR grey |
| Aircraft turn coordinator / "ball and needle" (turn-and-slip) | Kollsman, Sperry, Smiths | 1930s–present | aircraft | Black face, a white needle that swings left/right from a top pivot, a curved glass tube with a black ball underneath ("ball and needle"), "2 MIN" legend, white "L  R" | The needle-and-ball combination: a centre-zero needle plus a spirit-level ball — a hybrid F4/F10 |
| Soviet ГК-2 / Soviet tuner "Ласпи" / "Радиотехника Т-101" tuning | Радиотехника (Riga) | 1980s | Soviet tuner | Small square centre-zero meter with a Cyrillic "НАСТРОЙКА" | Cyrillic legend |

Build candidates: ★ Smiths/Lucas −30 0 +30 car ammeter, ★ FM centre-tune meter (Marantz 10B style, lit), ★ turn-and-slip needle-and-ball.

## F5 · Gauge, Bourdon-tube style (pressure, boiler, steam, diving)
| machine | maker / model | year | domain | what the readout looks like | why it's distinctive |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ★ Ashcroft steam gauge (Ashcroft Manufacturing Co., Bridgeport CT) | Ashcroft, ~6″ and 8½″ "railroad" and boiler gauges | 1852–1920s | steam boiler / locomotive | Cast brass case with a screwed brass bezel, thick bevelled glass, white enamel or paper dial with engraved black Didone/serif numerals in a full circle "POUNDS", ornate maker's cartouche in the centre ("ASHCROFT MFG. CO. NEW YORK"), a slim blued-steel spade pointer with a counterweight; a red hand-painted "working pressure" line on the dial | The brass bezel + white enamel dial + fancy Victorian lettering + red line; the Ashcroft eagle cartouche |
| ★ Bourdon / Schäffer & Budenberg (Magdeburg) manometer | Schaeffer & Budenberg; Bourdon-Sedeme (Paris) | 1850s–1930s | steam / industrial | Black japanned or brass case, silvered-brass dial with engraved numerals filled black, "ATM" or "kg/cm²", a delicate blued pointer; the S&B pressure-arch trademark | Silvered dial with engraved black fill; continental "ATM" scale |
| Crosby Steam Gage & Valve Co. | Crosby (Boston) | 1870s–1930s | locomotive / marine | 8″ brass case, dial in white enamel with black numerals and a **red** "hydrostatic test" band, "CROSBY" curved above the pivot | The tallest numerals of the American gauges; dark brass patina |
| Locomotive cab gauge cluster (Pennsylvania Railroad K4, LNER A4, NYC Hudson) | Ashcroft, Crosby, Smiths (UK), Dewrance (UK) | 1900–1950s | railway | Twin brass-bezelled 6″ gauges (boiler pressure, steam chest), white dials, red line at ~250 psi, lit by a small hooded lamp; British examples with "DEWRANCE" and black-bezel cases; LNER cab gauges with a "Dr" style typeface | The paired boiler-and-steam-chest gauges in a smoke-black cab |
| ★ US Navy fleet-submarine depth gauge (Gato/Balao class, USS Pampanito) | Kollsman / US Gauge (American Gauge Co.) | 1941–1945 | submarine | Large 8″ gauge, black dial with white numerals "FEET" 0–450 (or 0–600 on Balao), a fat white pointer with a white-outlined tail, brass or black bezel, a **red** arc at test depth; a second small "shallow" gauge 0–165 ft | The white-on-black depth face with the red-zone arc at the deep end; the control-room gauge board |
| Soviet submarine depth gauge (Project 641 "Foxtrot", Project 613 "Whiskey") | Soviet naval instrument works, глубиномер | 1950s–1980s | submarine | Black or dark-green dial with white Cyrillic "ГЛУБИНА, м", a wide brass bezel, a thick white pointer; often a cream-painted case | Cyrillic "м" and the cream-painted bezel |
| ★ Diving pressure gauge — US Navy Mark V helmet diving / Siebe Gorman (UK) | Siebe Gorman & Co; Morse Diving Equipment | 1900s–1960s | diving | Brass case, black-numeral dial "FEET OF SEA WATER" or "FATHOMS", the surface-supply pneumofathometer with two red pointers | The "fathoms" dial and the brass case with a hemp-rope-worn look |
| Scuba submersible pressure gauge (SPG) — US Divers / Aqua Lung, Scubapro | US Divers (Aqua Lung) 1960s–1980s; Scubapro | 1960s–present | diving | Small 2″ gauge on a rubber hose, black dial with a luminous green-white scale, "PSI" or "BAR", red reserve zone 0–500 psi, a chunky rubber boot | Red reserve arc and rubber boot |
| Bathysphere / Beebe-Barton depth gauge | Ashcroft (for Beebe & Barton, 1930–34) | 1930–1934 | deep sea | A brass Bourdon depth gauge in the sphere, white dial reading fathoms to 500+, mounted beside the quartz window | Historic; white dial in a cast-steel ball |
| ★ Fire-extinguisher / CO₂ / oxygen cylinder gauge (Victor, Harris regulator gauges) | Victor Equipment, Harris, Wika | 1930s–present | industrial gas | 2″ steel-cased gauge, white face with black numerals, a red "OVERPRESSURE" band, "USE NO OIL" in red across the dial, needle from the centre; on oxygen gauges a green tint | "USE NO OIL" on the face; the two gauges on a welding regulator |
| Tyre pressure / service-station air gauge (Schrader, Eco Air Tower) | Schrader; Eco Tireflator | 1920s–1960s | automotive | Big round glass-faced gauge on a red or green painted "Tireflator" tower, white dial 0–60 psi with a red pointer | The service-station tower gauge |
| Wika / Winters / Marsh Bellofram industrial gauge (modern generic) | Wika Type 111.10, Winters PEM | 1970s–present | industrial | Black steel or stainless case, white dial with black DIN numerals, "psi" outer / "kPa" inner in red, a short black needle; the small Wika triangle logo | The dual red/black scale (psi + kPa) |
| Boiler / heating steam gauge on domestic boilers (Winters "Tridicator") | Winters Instruments | 1950s–present | heating | Round gauge with two pointers: a black pressure needle and a red temperature needle on one dial ("TRIDICATOR": psi, feet of water, °F) | Two needles, three scales on one dial |
| Aircraft / machinery "Bourdon" oil pressure (Smiths, AC Delco, Stewart-Warner) | Stewart-Warner "Wings" series (1930s), Smiths Chronometric | 1930s–1950s | aircraft & automotive | 2″ black-dial gauge with white numerals, a white needle, chrome bezel; Stewart-Warner's "Wings" logo | See F6 |
| Barometer — Aneroid (Negretti & Zambra, Short & Mason "Stormoguide", Taylor) | Negretti & Zambra; Short & Mason "Stormoguide" (1920s); Taylor | 1880s–1950s | domestic / marine | Brass or oak-cased, silvered or paper dial with "STORMY · RAIN · CHANGE · FAIR · VERY DRY" in Victorian lettering, a black main pointer and a brass "set" hand you rotate by a knob in the glass | The word-scale and the second manually-set brass hand; the milky/silvered dial |

Build candidates: ★ Ashcroft brass boiler gauge (white enamel, Didone numerals, red line, cartouche), ★ US fleet-boat depth gauge (white-on-black, red test-depth arc), ★ welding regulator "USE NO OIL" gauge, ★ aneroid barometer with the manual brass set-hand, ★ scuba SPG with rubber boot.

## F6 · Automotive gauge (speedometer, tach, fuel, temp)
| machine | maker / model | year | domain | what the readout looks like | why it's distinctive |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Ford Model T / Model A speedometer (Stewart-Warner, Jones) | Stewart-Warner "Stewart" Model 26 (Model T, 1915–27), Model A oval (1928–31) | 1915–1931 | car | On the Model T a small round or drum-type speedometer with black numerals on a silvered dial in a nickel bezel; the Model A has an oval dash with a rotating drum speedo reading through a window (see A8) | The Model A oval nickel dash and its drum speedo |
| ★ 1936–39 Cord 810 / Auburn "engine-turned" dash | Cord (Auburn Automobile Co.) | 1936 | car | Aircraft-style black round gauges in an engine-turned (jewelled) aluminium panel; white numerals in a thin geometric sans; red needles | The engine-turned panel with a full row of aircraft dials |
| ★ 1953–59 Cadillac / 1957 Chevrolet Bel Air ribbon speedometer | GM (AC Spark Plug division) | 1953–1959 | car | A wide horizontal chrome-bordered window with a **red ribbon** (a rotating painted drum) that grows across a "0…120" scale; chrome bezels, gold-anodised numerals; the Bel Air's twin-cowl dash with a semicircular speedo | The red ribbon speedo (1959 Cadillac, 1958–60 Chevrolet Impala's arc is red too); chrome and gold |
| ★ 1961–66 Jaguar E-Type / Mk2 Smiths cluster | Smiths Industries (for Jaguar) | 1959–1969 | car | Black dials with white numerals in Smiths' Gill-ish sans, chrome bezels, a white or red needle from a centre boss; the E-Type's speedo reads to 160 mph, the tach with a red arc; a row of small round gauges (oil, amps, fuel, water) in a black-crackle centre panel with toggle switches under them | The Smiths black-dial-chrome-bezel look, the row of four small gauges above the toggles |
| Porsche 356 / 911 VDO | VDO (Vereinigte Deutsche Elektro-Werke), for Porsche | 1955–1989 | car | Five round black dials with white numerals in a DIN-like grotesque (later the "Porsche" sans), green-lit at night, the centre tach the biggest, a **red** zone on the tach; the 911's green numerals in the 1970s | The five-dial fan with the big centre tach; green night-glow on 70s cars |
| Mercedes-Benz W123 / W116 VDO | VDO | 1970s | car | Three dials, cream-white numerals on black, a red needle; the vertical strip fuel/temp/oil in the left dial | Mercedes' triple-dial with the vertical fuel bars |
| ★ Citroën DS / CX "bobbin" speedometer | Jaeger (for Citroën CX) | 1974–1985 (CX) | car | A rotating **drum** speedometer seen through a window under a curved lens, with the numerals on a cylinder that turns; the CX's "lunule" dash with drum speedo and tach and a big red index line | The CX rotating drum speedo — a unique reading |
| Volvo P1800 / Amazon | Smiths (Volvo P1800) | 1961–1973 | car | A horizontal "thermometer" ribbon speedometer on the P1800 (a red band that grows), plus round Smiths gauges | Another ribbon |
| 1970s Datsun 240Z / Toyota 2000GT | Nippon Seiki / Jeco | 1969–1975 | car | Three-dial hooded binnacle (240Z) with black faces, white Helvetica-style numerals, orange needles, "km/h" & "mph" dual scales | Orange needle on black, the deep hoods |

| BMW E30 / E28 | VDO / Motometer | 1980s | car | Black dials, white numerals in Helvetica, an orange-red needle, orange backlight; the "economy" gauge (a vacuum meter) beneath the speedo | Orange backlit BMW gauges |

| Pontiac / Chevrolet SS 1960s "Rally gauges" | AC Delco | 1960s | car | Small round gauges with a wide chrome bezel and a **conical** black-and-chrome centre; white lettering, red needle | The chrome cone |
| Smiths Chronometric tach/speedo (Vincent, Norton, Triumph) | Smiths | 1930s–1960s | motorcycle | Black dial, white numerals, chrome bezel; the chronometric's needle *steps* (it's a clockwork sampling mechanism) | The stepping needle |
