## F7 · Aircraft instrument (altimeter, airspeed, artificial horizon, VSI)
| machine | maker / model | year | domain | what the readout looks like | why it's distinctive |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ★ Kollsman sensitive altimeter (Type C-12, later AN-5760) | Kollsman Instrument Co., Elmhurst NY | 1928–1950s | aircraft | 3⅛″ black dial with white numerals 0–9 around, **three hands** (a long thin hundreds, a short fat thousands, a stubby ten-thousands with a small triangle), a **Kollsman window** at 3 o'clock showing the barometric setting in inches Hg (29.92), a knurled knob at 7 o'clock; a striped (crosshatch) flag below 10,000 ft on later types | The three-hand altimeter is the canonical aircraft dial; the crosshatched "low altitude" flag |
| Smiths Mk.XVII / Mk.14 altimeter (RAF Spitfire, Lancaster) | Smiths Aircraft Instruments; Kelvin Hughes | 1930s–1950s | RAF aircraft | Black dial, white numerals in the Air Ministry "AM" stencil-ish sans, three hands, a millibar sub-dial in a window; "AIR MINISTRY" and a crown stamp on the case | The RAF grey-black panel with the millibar window and the AM stamp |
| ★ Airspeed indicator (Kollsman / Pioneer / US Navy F4U, P-51) | Kollsman; Pioneer Instrument (Bendix) | 1940s | aircraft | Black dial "AIR SPEED" with white "KNOTS" or "MPH" 0–700 in a non-linear sweep, coloured arcs on later civil ones (white flap arc, green normal, yellow caution, **red line**), a single white needle | The coloured arc scheme; the "red line" |
| Sperry A-12 / Sperry Directional Gyro & Artificial Horizon | Sperry Gyroscope Co. | 1930s–1950s | aircraft | Artificial horizon: a black case, a **gyro-stabilised horizon bar** (white line across a blue-over-brown or black-over-black card) with a fixed miniature aeroplane symbol, a bank scale at top with a pointer; an OFF flag | The blue sky / brown earth ball; the miniature aeroplane |
| ★ Soviet АГД-1 / АГБ-3К attitude indicator (MiG-21, Su-7) | Soviet aviation instruments | 1950s–1980s | aircraft | A blue/brown (or black/blue) sphere with a white aircraft symbol, Cyrillic labels, a red "flag"; on the АГД the horizon ball works inversely to Western (the aircraft symbol moves, the horizon is fixed) | Inverted Soviet presentation; Cyrillic; a characteristic gold-grey case |
| ★ Vertical Speed Indicator (VSI) — Kollsman / United Instruments | Kollsman | 1940s–present | aircraft | Black dial "VERTICAL SPEED" in a symmetrical up/down scale, "0" at 9 o'clock, "UP" and "DOWN" in a curve, one white needle, "100 FEET PER MINUTE" | The 0-at-the-left symmetric layout |
| Turn & bank / turn coordinator (see F4) | Sperry, Kollsman, Kelvin Hughes | 1930s–present | aircraft | Needle plus a ball in a curved glass tube; on the turn coordinator, a miniature aeroplane that banks | Needle + ball |
| Manifold pressure / RPM (P-51, B-17 engine gauges) | Kollsman; Autolite | 1940s | aircraft engine | 2″ black dial with two needles (twin-engine) or a single white needle, "INCHES HG", "RPM ×100" | The twin needles marked 1 and 2 |
| ★ Boeing 707 / DC-8 flight-deck (Kollsman & Bendix "Type 2 & 3" era, the "steam gauge" panel) | Bendix / Kollsman / Sperry | 1958–1970s | airliner | Rows of 3″ black round-in-square instruments with white markings; the Collins FD-108 flight director with a **blue-over-black ball**; the ADF and RMI with dual pointers | The "steam gauge" cockpit |
| Concorde / Vickers VC10 Smiths instruments | Smiths Industries | 1960s–1970s | airliner | Smiths' black instruments with slightly narrower, more condensed white numerals and a blue-grey bezel | Smiths' condensed face |
| ★ WWII German Luftwaffe instruments (Fl. 22320 Fahrtmesser, Fl. 22561 Wendezeiger, Askania) | Askania, Bruhn, R. Fuess | 1935–1945 | aircraft | Black dial, white or luminous numerals in **DIN 1451-Engschrift**, "Fl." Werknummer stencil on the case; the airspeed "km/h"; the Kollsman-style knob; a green-brown bakelite case | DIN typeface on a black dial is the Luftwaffe look |
| Japanese A6M Zero instruments (Tokyo Aircraft Instrument Co.) | Tokyo Koku Keiki | 1940–1945 | aircraft | Black dials with kanji and Arabic numerals, brass-coloured bezels | Kanji instrument faces |
| Attitude "8-ball" — Apollo FDAI (Flight Director Attitude Indicator) | Honeywell (for NASA) | 1966–1972 | spacecraft | A grey-and-black 3D ball with a white lattice of pitch/roll lines, red "gimbal lock" zones, three error needles, mounted in the DSKY-grey panel | The Apollo 8-ball |
| Soviet Vostok / Soyuz "Globus" IMP navigation indicator | Soviet | 1961–2002 | spacecraft | A rotating globe under a glass with an aircraft symbol — an F8 hybrid | The Globus |
| Helicopter (Bell 47 / UH-1) engine cluster | Bendix, Weston | 1950s–1970s | aircraft | Dual-needle gauges (rotor and engine RPM on one dial with two needles, "R" and "E") | The R/E dual-needle tach |

Build candidates: ★ Kollsman three-hand altimeter with Kollsman window and crosshatch flag, ★ Airspeed indicator with white/green/yellow/red arcs, ★ artificial horizon (blue/brown ball with miniature aeroplane), ★ VSI symmetric 0-at-left, ★ Luftwaffe DIN-Engschrift Fahrtmesser, ★ Apollo FDAI 8-ball (stretch).

| ★ Directional gyro (Sperry, Kollsman / "heading indicator") | Sperry, Kollsman | 1930s–present | aircraft | A round instrument with a rotating card in white on black, a fixed aircraft outline in the centre pointing up; a "PUSH TO CAGE" knob at bottom left | The card turns behind a fixed plane symbol |
| Horizontal Situation Indicator (HSI) — Collins 331A-3G, King KI-525A | Collins | 1960s–1990s | aircraft | Compass card with a course arrow through the centre, a deviation bar, TO/FROM flags, a heading bug (orange), "NAV" and "HDG" flags | The HSI's card + course arrow + orange bug |
| RMI / ADF indicator | Bendix, Collins | 1950s–1970s | aircraft | A rotating card with two pointers (single-bar and double-bar) | The two-pointer RMI |
| Military lensatic compass (US M-1950, Cammenga 3H) | Cammenga; Stocker & Yale | 1950–present | infantry | Aluminium (later green plastic) case with a rotating card in luminous (tritium) green-on-black, a sight wire and lens, a bezel that clicks 3° per stop | The luminous green-on-black card and the clicking bezel |
| Silva Ranger / Suunto A-30 / Recta DP | Silva (Sweden), 1932– | 1932–present | orienteering | A clear plastic baseplate, a liquid capsule with a rotating bezel, a red-white needle in a "north box"; red "orienting arrow" | The clear baseplate |
| Car dashboard compass (1950s Airguide, Dinsmore, VDO) | Airguide (Chicago) | 1950s–1970s | automotive | A small dome with a suspended card on the dash, with a chrome base | The dome |
| Pocket / marching compass — Francis Barker M-73 prismatic (British Army) | F. Barker & Son | 1920s–present | military | A brass case, a liquid-filled card with a luminous mother-of-pearl-white rose and a black-on-white degree ring, the lid's prism | The mother-of-pearl card |
| ★ Soviet КИ-13 / Soviet marine magnetic compass КМ-145 | Soviet | 1950s–1980s | aircraft / marine | Aircraft drum compass with Cyrillic "С Ю В З" instead of N S E W | The Cyrillic cardinal points |

| machine | maker / model | year | domain | what the readout looks like | why it's distinctive |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ★ RCA 6E5 / 6U5 "Magic Eye" (round, end-viewed) | RCA; tube type 6E5 (1935), 6U5/6G5 | 1935–1950s | radio | A round green phosphor target ~1″ dia. seen end-on through a hole in the dial escutcheon, a **dark wedge shadow** that narrows as signal strengths; a black centre cap (the cathode shield) | The original "cat's-eye": green ring with a closing pie-shaped shadow |

| Telefunken / Philips EM4 / EM11 (the "fan" eye) | Telefunken, Philips | 1938–1950s | German radio | Round with four fan-shaped shadow sectors (EM11) | The 4-sector fan |
| ★ EM84 / 6FG6 (bar/"band" type, side-viewed) | Philips / Mullard; Telefunken; Soviet 6Е1П is the 6E5 type, 6Е3П ≈ EM84 | 1959–1970s | radio, tape recorder (Grundig TK series, Telefunken Magnetophon, Revox G36) | A narrow horizontal green bar with **two bright wedges growing toward the centre** from the ends, seen through a slot; used as a recording level indicator on tape decks | The two lit bars meeting in the middle: the Grundig recorder look |
| EM80 / EM81 / EM85 (round, side-viewed "fan") | Philips / Mullard; Telefunken | 1953–1960s | radio (Grundig, Saba, Telefunken, Nordmende) | A circular green face with a fan or a pair of shadows, viewed from the side through a round hole | The German "Magisches Auge" — Grundig "Konzertgerät" with the eye in a chrome-ringed hole |
| EM87 (bar type, dual-scale) | Telefunken | 1960s | Braun and Grundig tape | Bar type with a finer, more linear bar | The precision bar |
| Soviet 6Е5С / 6Е1П | Soviet | 1950s–1970s | Soviet radio (Ригонда, Латвия, Рекорд) | A green round eye in the Riga-built radios' walnut cabinets, often behind a small chromed escutcheon | Cyrillic escutcheon |
| Nixie? no. Tape decks with EM84: Revox G36, Uher 4000L (?), Tandberg 64 | Revox (Studer), Tandberg | 1960s | reel-to-reel | The bar eye behind a small rectangular window in a brushed panel | Bar eye in a rectangular window |
| Modern kits: the 6E5 eye in Chinese "magic eye" VU kits, 6E2 | | 2010s | hobby | Two 6E2 tubes as stereo level meters | Reference only |

---

## Notes for Phase 3

- **Typefaces observed** (for the Styles phase): Weston / Simpson scale figures are a condensed engraver's grotesque; Sifam and Ernest Turner use Gill Sans or Univers; Smiths (Jaguar, RAF) a narrow Gill-like grotesque; VDO and Luftwaffe instruments DIN 1451 (Engschrift on wartime); Solari flaps and Cifra 3 Helvetica; Casio LCD a slanted 7-segment; HP bubble LEDs an ultra-thin-stroke 7-seg; Soviet instruments a Cyrillic grotesque with GOST class symbols; Ashcroft/Crosby steam gauges Didone (Bodoni/Scotch Roman) engraved figures; Toledo scales a slab-serif enamel.
- **Colour anchors**: Weston buff (VU), McIntosh blue, Marantz blue, Sansui amber, Nixie orange-red (~#ff6a1a with a pink halo), Panaplex orange (lighter, ~#ff8a3c), VFD cyan-green (~#7dffd6 through smoked glass), red LED (~#ff2a1a, HP thinner and deeper), plasma orange (~#ff7a1e on near-black), flip-dot yellow-green (~#d6e600), Ferranti-Packard black, EM84 phosphor green (~#7bffa0), Telechron ivory, Solari black.
- **Cross-references**: F4/F7/F10 share the turn-and-slip ball; F13 and G1 share the cyclometer drum; G6 and G15 share Burroughs SELF-SCAN; G3 and G13 share manual tin-plate scoreboards; G7 note that the Yamaha DX7 is an LCD (G9), not a VFD; G10 note that the pinball DMD is plasma (G15), not LED.


---

# H · Lamps and light, I · Mechanical and fluid, J · Panels and housings

*Phase 2 of `../PLAN.md`. For each code in H, I and J: the machines across history that carried a memorable, recognisable example. Years marked ~ are approximate. ★ marks the entries most promising as web mock-ups (distinctive, visually rich, feasible in CSS/SVG). Notes on accuracy: where the original used a fluorescent (VFD) bargraph rather than true LEDs, it is filed under H6, not H5.*

---

## H1 · Pilot lamp with jewel lens
| machine | maker / model | year | domain | what it looks like | why it's distinctive |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Fender Tweed / Blackface / Silverface amps (Deluxe, Bassman, Twin Reverb) | Fender; jewel by Leecraft / later Fender-branded | 1955– | guitar amp | ~1" faceted red (later amber, blue, green) glass or plastic jewel in a chrome-plated brass fluted housing; on tweed amps sits on the chrome control plate next to "PILOT" | the deep-cut facets (a "dome cut" of ~24 facets) throw a starburst; the jewel is the one point of colour on an otherwise silver plate; the blackface jewel is slightly larger and the lamp inside is a #47 bayonet whose filament you can see when it's off |
| Dialight 0930 / 081 / 095 series panel indicators | Dialight (Brooklyn) | 1940s–1980s | industrial / mil / test | ½" or ¾" faceted domed lens in red, green, amber, white, blue; screw-in "jewel" over a T-3¼ bayonet lamp; polished nickel or black anodised bezel with a knurled ring | the catalogue jewel of American panels — the knurled ring in a hex-nut mounting on the back of a grey wrinkle panel; lenses interchangeable so you see mixed colours in a row |
| Leecraft pilot light assemblies | Leecraft Mfg (NY) | 1940s–60s | radio / amp / industrial | small chrome bezel with a fluted or plain jewel; Leecraft made the jewel Fender used; "Leecraft" cast on the socket | slimmer chrome bezel than Dialight; the plastic jewel that has yellowed and crazed on old amps |
| Hallicrafters SX-28 / SX-42 / S-38 receivers | Hallicrafters | 1940–1950s | ham radio | small faceted red or green jewel in a chrome ring on a black wrinkle panel beside the "DIAL LIGHT"/"SEND-RECEIVE" legend; some with flat "cat's eye" rectangular jewel | the jewel colour is the only colour on a black-wrinkle-and-chrome panel; the jewel is often mounted on the dial escutcheon |
| Collins KWM-2 / 75S-3 / 32S-3 | Collins Radio | 1959–1970s | ham radio | light grey ("St James Grey") wrinkle panel, small red or green dome pilot in a plain chrome bezel next to the silver-black dial | the small restrained jewel on a tidy, elegant grey panel — the American professional look |
| McIntosh MC275 / MC240 amplifiers | McIntosh | 1961– | hi-fi | small red jewel pilot on the chrome chassis top plate between the transformer cans; black-glass front-panel indicators on the preamps | red jewel on polished chrome, the "McIntosh chrome chassis" look |
| Simpson 260 / Triplett 630 accessories, Heathkit IT-series testers | Heathkit | 1950s–70s | test | Heathkit used ¼" or ⅜" faceted red or amber jewel (often the "Chicago Miniature" or Dialight types) on hammertone-grey or two-tone green panels, with "ON" in white silk-screen | pairs with the Heathkit Heath-green/grey panels; the jewel in a plain chrome bezel, no knurling |
| Western Electric / Bell System switchboards & 1A2 key telephone lamps | Western Electric | 1920s–1970s | telephony | rows of tiny ½" jewelled lamp caps (the 2-type lamp cap) in clear, red, green, amber, "opal white"; flat domed, ribbed edge, pressed into a jack strip | the row of dozens of small jewelled caps under each jack — the "switchboard" look; the ribbed edge to the cap |
| Ford Model A / 1930s dashboard indicators; 1950s Chevrolet "GEN" and "OIL" jewels | Ford, GM | 1928–1950s | automotive | small red jewels in the dash face for oil pressure / generator; later chrome-bezelled amber jewel for high-beam ("BEAM") | the round red jewel in a chrome bezel set in a painted steel dash; the high-beam indicator's blue jewel |
| Bakelite fuse boxes / industrial motor starters (Allen-Bradley, Square D, Cutler-Hammer) | Allen-Bradley 800T series | 1950s–90s | industrial | 30 mm "full voltage" pilot lights: a domed 1" lens (red, green, amber, blue, white) in a chrome bezel, often "push-to-test"; mounted through a grey steel enclosure | the big 30 mm oil-tight dome with a shallow faceted or plain lens; the "PUSH TO TEST" ring |
| Soviet СЛ-type signal lamps / АЛ-307 with ФРМ lenses; "Сигнальная арматура" on Soviet radio sets and test gear | USSR (Ленинградский завод и др.) | 1950s–80s | Soviet mil / lab | small black or grey Bakelite or aluminium body with a domed ribbed lens in red, green, yellow (often a "wavy" lens with concentric ribs rather than facets); Cyrillic "СЕТЬ" (mains) legend | the ribbed rather than faceted lens; the black phenolic body; "СЕТЬ" painted or engraved |
| Marshall JTM45 / JMP / 1959 Super Lead | Marshall | 1962–1970s | guitar amp | small red domed pilot jewel (Bulgin or Arcolectric) in a black bezel on the gold Plexiglas panel | the jewel pattern (Arcolectric's lens is domed and lightly faceted) on gold; the "mains / stand-by" pair of toggles beside it |
| Bulgin D676 / Arcolectric panel neon and lamp jewels | Bulgin (UK), Arcolectric (UK) | 1940s–70s | British radio / amp / lab | ¾" domed jewel in a chrome bezel with a black or red plastic dome, the Bulgin with a distinctive dome-of-many-small-facets like a raspberry | the British look: rounder, more bulbous, a smaller bezel; the "raspberry" facet pattern |
| Vox AC30 | Vox / JMI | 1958– | guitar amp | small red jewel in a chrome ring on the copper (later black) control panel | the copper panel with a single red jewel |
| Ampex 350 / 351 tape machine electronics | Ampex | 1954–1960s | studio | small red jewel pilot beside the VU on the grey-green panel | the jewel in the same panel as the VU meter |
| Hammond B-3 (power switch pilot on the "Run/Start" plate) | Hammond | 1955–1974 | organ | small red jewel next to the START / RUN toggles on the brown Bakelite plate | the red jewel set into brown Bakelite with cream legends |

Build candidates: ★ Fender blackface jewel (chrome fluted bezel, deep facets, #47 filament visible), ★ Dialight ¾" jewel with knurled ring on grey wrinkle, ★ Western Electric switchboard jewelled cap strip (row of small domes), ★ Soviet ribbed-lens СЕТЬ lamp on black phenolic, ★ Allen-Bradley 30 mm push-to-test dome.

| Soyuz / Vostok "Сигнал" and "ИнПУ" panels; Buran | USSR (НИИ приборостроения) | 1961–1990s | spaceflight | rows of square white-lettered black tiles lit green/amber/red; Cyrillic bold grotesque ("АВАРИЯ", "ГЛАВНАЯ"); the rounded-corner tile with a small square lamp; grey-green panel | Cyrillic legends in a heavy grotesk; often a lit tile beside a mechanical globe or a "Глобус" indicator |
| Nuclear control rooms: Three Mile Island TMI-2 (Babcock & Wilcox / Bailey), Westinghouse plants, Sizewell B, Soviet RBMK (Chernobyl unit control) | B&W, Westinghouse, Bailey Meter | 1960s–80s | power | large ceiling-high panels of 2"×4" (or 1"×2") annunciator "windows" in a grid (e.g. 6 rows × 20), each a white translucent tile with the alarm printed in black (Helvetica or Univers), lit amber/white/red from behind; flashing when unacknowledged, steady when acknowledged; "ACK", "TEST", "RESET" buttons below | the massive grid of rectangular windows — the Panalarm / Ronan "annunciator" — the tile that flashes and the horn; TMI's ~750 alarms in the first minutes |
| Panalarm 90 series; Ronan X11 series; Beacon "Rochester Instrument" annunciators | Panalarm (Ametek), Ronan | 1950s– | industrial | modular annunciator: tiles in rows of 4/8/12 in a grey steel bezel; each window ~2"×3" with a black-on-white film legend; lamps behind; first-out logic | THE industrial annunciator — a grey bezel with a grid of white windows |

| Raytheon / Kelvin Hughes / Sperry Marine 1960s–80s | Kelvin Hughes | 1960s–80s | marine | Kelvin Hughes Type 14/17: a "true motion" display in a pedestal with a big hood | the pedestal-hood radar |

| Laboratory mercury thermometers (Brannan, Amarell, Ertco) | Brannan | 1900s– | lab | plain glass stem with a yellow-backed capillary and etched marks, mercury or blue/red spirit; "N.P.L." certification | the yellow-backed capillary |

## I12 · Flag / bat indicator on a valve, the OPEN–SHUT plate

## J10 · Legends — engraved lamacoid, silk-screen, Dymo, decal, hot-stamp, etched brass

| Engraved lamacoid / "Gravoply" plates on rack gear and telco: Neve 80-series module ID plates and studio patch-bay strips (the 1073's own legends are silk-screened on grey-blue enamel), Western Electric bays (black lamacoid with white letters, 1940s–70s), Ampex 350 (engraved), aircraft placards (white-on-black lamacoid, engraved, rounded corners, two screws), studio patch bays (black lamacoid strips with white engraved channel names), Tektronix serial plates; mil gear ("MIL-P-15024" plates) | Western Electric, Bell Labs, Boeing | 1940s– | telco / aircraft / studio | a two-layer phenolic or acrylic plate (black over white, or white over black; sometimes red over white) with legends engraved through the top layer with a rotary engraver in a monoline "Gravograph" sans (uniform stroke, rounded terminals, sometimes "Leroy"-like), rounded corners, two small screws or double-sided tape; on aircraft: white letters on black, "PULL TO RESET" | the monoline rotary-engraved sans with rounded terminals — the "engraved plate" look; the two-layer edge showing the underlayer at the plate edge |
| Silk-screened legends on Japanese hi-fi (Technics, Pioneer, Sansui, Marantz 1970s: black Helvetica on brushed aluminium; Sony's 1980s: white on black; Yamaha's fine white; Roland's orange "Roland" and black Helvetica on silver; Boss's white on enamel with "Roland" black), on Tektronix (blue/black on cream: Tek's Helvetica-based), on Braun (Akzidenz-Grotesk, tiny), on HP (Helvetica / Univers on beige); on Moog (white "Futura"-ish? — Moog used a Futura-like on the Minimoog, the "MOOG" logo), on ARP (white Helvetica on black), on Korg (MS-20: white on black with the "Korg" script), on Buchla (a Univers on blue) | Technics, Roland, Braun, Tektronix, Moog | 1960s–80s | hi-fi / synth / test | thin-film printed ink (matte black, matte white, or a colour) slightly raised, with crisp edges; wears through where thumbs land (the "TREBLE" legend on a well-used amp is half gone); the hi-fi's sub-mm hairline "index" lines around a knob; Roland's orange stripe | the crisp printed Helvetica; the wear pattern under the thumb; Roland's orange line |
| Dymo embossed tape (1958–; ⅜"/½" tape in black, red, blue, green — a white-embossed capital letter on a coloured vinyl tape, stuck onto gear): studio racks (Abbey Road, the BBC), Moog modular patch panels, 1970s computer rooms, the "Dymo'd" Fender Rhodes, Eventide racks; the 1960s "Dymo M-5 / M-10" labelmakers | Dymo Industries | 1958–80s | everywhere | a strip of glossy vinyl (black, red, blue, green, yellow) with white capitals raised in emboss, each letter with the characteristic "pressed" halo, straight-cut or rounded ends (later), often slightly crooked, peeling at one end | the raised white capitals on black tape — the label of the studio |
| Hot-stamp (foil stamping) on Bakelite / plastic: Hammond plates (cream on brown), 1950s telephone dials, Soviet radios' gold hot-stamp on black (ВЭФ, Океан), Japanese 1970s calculators' silver hot-stamp, the Commodore 64's? (silk-screen), toys, the "MADE IN JAPAN" on the back of a Walkman, Roland Juno's? (silk-screen); hot-stamped Fender knob numerals (the numerals on a Fender amp knob 1–10 are hot-stamped white) | Hammond, VEF, Fender | 1930s–80s | domestic / music | foil pressed into the plastic with heat, so the letters are very slightly recessed with a bright metallic (gold, silver) or flat (white, cream) finish; edges slightly soft; wears off the raised parts first | the recessed metallic gold on a Soviet black radio; the cream on Hammond brown; the white numerals on a Fender knob |
| Etched / engraved brass (J5): the Chadburn's telegraph, Victorian instruments (a silvered brass scale with black-filled engraving in a fine copperplate or an Egyptian), railway brass plates, clock dials (silvered, engraved), the "builder's plate" (Baldwin Locomotive Works, cast, not etched), the etched brass nameplate on a Bugatti; the "etched and filled" (black wax) | Chadburn's, Negretti & Zambra | 1850s–1930s | marine / science | fine engraved lines filled with black wax on a silvered or lacquered brass surface; a Clarendon or a copperplate script; the "crazing" of the silvering | the black-filled engraving on silvered brass |

## J11 · Fasteners and furniture — screws, rivets, grommets, hinges, handles, feet, grilles, vents, cable glands
| machine | maker / model | year | domain | what it looks like | why it's distinctive |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Screw heads by era: slotted (everything to ~1940; still on British/German gear to the 1960s: Grundig's slotted, brass wood screws on radio cabinets, "cheese-head" slotted machine screws on Leica), Phillips (patented 1936, Cadillac 1936, standard on US wartime and postwar gear: Tektronix, Fender, Ampex — the cross with its tapered slot), Pozidriv (1962, on European/British gear: the extra tick marks; Dual, Revox), JIS cross (Japanese: Sony, Technics — a Phillips-like with a dot mark, that Phillips drivers cam out of), Robertson square (Canada, 1908), Torx (1967; on 1980s+ automotive, Apple 2008+ Pentalobe), Allen/socket cap (1910s; on 1970s–80s synths: Moog's set screws, Prophet's hex bolts; Eurorack's black M3 hex), Bristol spline (on Collins knobs), knurled-head thumbscrews (test gear, Nagra's, the Tektronix plug-in's), black oxide vs zinc vs stainless vs "cad-plated" gold (military "cadmium yellow") | Phillips Screw Co., Tektronix, Sony, Collins | 1900s– | everywhere | the head's drive recess (slot / cross / Pozi ticks / JIS dot / Torx star / hex socket), the head shape (pan, round, oval/countersunk, cheese, button, truss), the finish (black oxide dull, zinc bluish-silver, cad-plated iridescent yellow, nickel bright, brass), a burr where a driver slipped; on Bakelite radios, the brass slotted screw with a cup washer; on a Fender chassis, nickel Phillips pan-heads | the correct head type for the era — slotted pre-war, Phillips postwar US, Pozi Europe, JIS Japan, Torx modern, hex on synths; the cad-plated yellow on military |
| Rack ears and rack screws: the 19" EIA rack (1922, AT&T), 10-32 and 12-24 rack screws with black nylon cup washers (the 1980s black rack), the "rack ear" — a folded steel flange with two slotted holes; Neve modules' knurled thumbscrews; Tektronix's "TM500" plug-in latch | AT&T, Tektronix | 1922– | studio / test | a black-anodised 1U panel with four rack holes in slots, black 10-32 screws with cup washers; a rack rail with square holes (1990s cage nuts) or tapped holes (earlier) | the rack ear slot and the black cup-washer screw |
| Dzus fasteners (1932, William Dzus) — quarter-turn fasteners on aircraft panels (the slotted button with a spring wire underneath), on race cars (the "Dzus button" with a curved slot), on the Fender amp? (no — screws), on 1960s–70s military electronics (the quarter-turn on a PRC-77), Camloc (1940s, the flush "Camloc" on jets), the "quarter-turn" on hi-fi? ## Notes for Phase 3

- **Richest veins in this slice** (most distinctive + most feasible): the annunciator family (H3: Boeing glareshield, Apollo C&W, Panalarm nuclear grid, PDP-11/70, Soyuz), CRT phosphor (H12: Tek 465 internal graticule vs Tek 545 edge-lit external, the 2465's beam-drawn readout, the ECG erase bar), radar PPI (H13: Decca two-colour persistence, SAGE, Furuno raster), reels (I5: Ampex NAB hub, IBM 729 vacuum columns), chart recorders (I8: Esterline-Angus, the circular Honeywell-Brown chart, the helicorder drum, the barograph), and every J code — J1 (Braun vs Marantz vs Minimoog brushed), J2 (Tek hammertone vs Bell & Howell wrinkle vs Soviet sage), J10 (lamacoid, Dymo, hot-stamp, stencil, DIN, Cyrillic, Eurostile).
- **Careful distinctions the builders should keep**: true LED bargraphs (H5: Sony TC-K71, Dorrough, Fostex, dbx, LM3915) vs fluorescent/VFD bars (H6: Pioneer Fluoroscan, Technics FL, Sony ES) — different glow, different colour; brushed (directional grain: Braun, Technics) vs bead-blasted satin (Apple); hammertone (dimples: Tek 500, Heathkit, Soviet) vs wrinkle/crackle (raised web: Bell & Howell, Hallicrafters black) vs smooth enamel (Ampex grey-green, Bridgeport); Bakelite (brown/black, opaque, glossy) vs Catalin (marbled, translucent, saturated) vs Soviet карболит (matte chocolate) vs urea (cream, opaque); slotted/Phillips/Pozi/JIS/Torx/hex by era and country.
- **Crossovers to other slices**: H7 lit legends ↔ D3 illuminated pushbuttons and G13 flags; I4 semaphore ↔ B4 quadrant levers and C14 signal levers; I7 platter strobe ↔ A11 jog wheels; I12 valve indicators ↔ C16 cam switches and A12 handwheels; J8 dial glass ↔ A7 slide-rule dials and H11 backlights; J10 legends is the typography source for every code.
